# Artifacts: what was published, and where it lives

Three kinds of artifact came out of this work: **published reports** (HTML, deployed to spacesheep.dev),
**repostable images**, and **tools** that can be re-run. Commits are listed at the end so any artifact can be
tied to the state of the tree that produced it. Cite as **A1**...**A25** (A9 and A10 were never assigned):
A1–A5, A11, A13–A15, A17 and A19 are reports · A6–A8 images · A12 math rendering · A16 the rerun tools · A18 the
wire tools · A20 the session timeline, page and tools · A21 the sparse parity page and its workload · A22 the heatsink feasibility study · A23 the transistor-heat timeline, its page, data and builder · A24 the matmul-to-transistor chain page, its data block and builder · A25 the kinds-of-numbers page (Q130(k)). The public index
of every report is the hub, [Limits of observability](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-limits-of-observability#reports) (A2).

Reports A2–A5, A11, A13–A15 and A17 are assembled by `scripts/build-report.py <name> <data.json> <out.html>` from
`docs/reports/sources/<name>.{meta.json,body.html,script.js}` (A2 also keeps its data in
`limits-of-observability.data.json`, whose computed blocks `python3 tools/ettelem/sync_hub_data.py` writes: run it
before `build-report.py`, and again after regenerating any file it reads; `--check` exits 1 if the data file is
stale; A16 lists the blocks). Edit the sources, not the HTML: it is regenerated. A1 is generated by
`python3 workloads/memprobe/build_report.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-19-memprobe-aifoundry2/summary.json
docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/manual.json docs/reports/2026-09-19-et-soc1-memory-anatomy.html` from
`workloads/memprobe/report_template.html` (E1 gives the whole chain; its `--v3` input, the three cards' latencies, comes
from `workloads/memprobe/analyze.py --v3`, "Rebuilding the version-3 data" below): edit the template, not the HTML.
A19 and the four 2026-09-18 reports are hand-written HTML whose embedded JSON is rewritten by their `--embed`
scripts (the commands are in "Rebuilding the standalone pages" below). The 2026-09-18 test drive
(`docs/report/index.html`) is hand-written HTML whose `ladder-data` JSON `scripts/mmbench-report-data.py --ladder`
rewrites; the two 2026-09-22 briefs are plain hand-written HTML (the L2 brief is now a pointer page). For these, edit
the prose in the HTML. Every page with charts carries the shared chart toolkit (A12): the source-built pages get it
from `scripts/build-report.py`, and the standalone pages from `scripts/paste-chartkit.py PAGE`, which refreshes the
marked block and changes nothing else (`--check` reports a missing or stale block).

**A25 · Multiplying different numbers: speed, heat and power** (31 October 2026, Q130(k)). The blog follow-up
material on one page: Horace He's effect beside the ET-SoC-1's, then one generated table of all 51 kinds of numbers ×
fp32 / fp16 / int8 (switching power, energy per op and per multiply-add, burst and sustained clock, TFLOPS or TOPS, the
die's rise and the limiting test, with estimates marked apart from measurements), and sections on speed, heat, power,
the formats, zeros, transistors, the literature and what is open, with three charts. The table and the charts are
generated from this repository's committed data (E78, E80, E83 and the Q130 desk directories, read at commit
`734e997f`; since the rebuild below, these and E84, E86, E87, E89, E91 and the real tiles, read at `803fd16a`) by a
chart builder that lives, with the page's source, in the owner's private research log rather than in
this repository; the page is recorded here because every figure on it cites a file here. Space
`8e61a8e0-f541-4d1e-87b2-15ee2a335e37`, public, unlisted, linked from
[the Horace hub](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-hub). First deployed at 16:37 PDT (`f94dca088ed2`, then
`71fdb0e4dbff` at 16:40), corrected after its verifier and redeployed at 17:30 PDT as `759156cadf12`: the zero gate with
its A side (one zero A word gates a whole micro-op), the leakage loop's gain scoped to the minute-long runs, five
idle-reset burst cells marked with their reset-free means, and E82's missed band. **Rebuilt in place at 22:53 PDT as
`df55db86ada8`** (Q130(p), over `759156cadf12`, which still named E84 and E85 "running, results to follow"): E84 on
card 0 beside aifoundry2 in a generated cross-card speed table, E85's camera per kind, E88's split, E89's fine heat
reading, E90's signs, and a section on the real tensors (Q130(n), E86, E87, E91) with its own chart; the kinds table
gained nine GPT-2 rows. Its verifier's wording fixes followed in place at about 23:32 PDT as `9729b0fe0afe`
(every real-tensor ratio names its baseline; the real-tensor section is scoped to three GPT-2 tiles). Still public
and unlisted. The claims for those experiments are in [05-claims.md](05-claims.md), "Q130 additions". **Rebuilt in place on
Sun 1 Nov 2026 (week 44) at 09:54 PST as `1fe8377a18af`** (Q132, over `9729b0fe0afe`; its data pinned at lab `17cc3911`,

**A24 · From matmul speed to one transistor switch** (27 October 2026, Q128). The nine-step chain on one
page: speed, the governor, board watts, picojoules per multiply-add, femtojoules per flipped operand bit,
net toggles, gate and wire capacitance, one transistor's gate, electrons and Landauer's floor — each rung
with its number, its evidence, its confidence and what is missing, and a waterfall that draws the two thirds
of a random multiply-add's energy the bottom-up count cannot yet name. Built by `scripts/build-report.py`
from `docs/reports/sources/matmul-to-transistor.{meta.json,body.html,script.js}` and the data block
`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-matmul-to-transistor/data.json`, which `build_data.py <repo root> <out.json>`
in that directory regenerates by reading every figure out of a scored experiment's own committed JSON — so
the page cannot drift from the record, and a correction to a data directory changes the page on rebuild.
Two figures are literals in the builder with their source named there (E68's price band, chain-closure's
bottom-up burst range). Space `5576af71-35da-4958-b437-aba8d59e2ee0`, public, unlisted. The page's script
also renders an optional `vault` block of extra boxes when its data carries one; nothing in this repository
writes that block and the committed data has none.
**Rebuilt in place twice on 28 October 2026 for Q129** — the same space, slug and visibility, version
`976cd5ad3f3e` at 20:31Z replacing `4ca541df9c3e`, then `e29a5cd2398c` at 20:54Z — with a measured
board-to-rail step, routed wires, every farad priced, a measured glitch factor, the lane's transport, and
E74–E76 folded in. Section 3's ledger table gained the regulators' 0.875 pJ row and **two** closures against
the board (bottom-up rows only, 34% → 69–72%, and 84–88% with that row counted, each labelled), so its bottom
rows follow from the rows above them; the glitch step is published split into its measured switching half and
its scaled internal half; the shared CTS root is named as a second ceiling on the transport range; and the
footer now says **which half of the page is read from JSON** — 26 figures across six Q129 lane directories are
re-checked on every build and the build fails if one is absent, while the kernels block is read whole from
`rf.json`, `mf.json` and `tr.json` and the rest of the Q129 block stays transcribed from the directory named on
each line. Every registered prediction that failed is printed on the page. `check_page.sh` ran clean at 1,280
and 390 px in both themes after each rebuild (0 JS errors, 0 bad anchors, 0 empty fields, 94 `q-*` ids with no
duplicates, all 61 `data-q` keys set), and `check-mirror.py --via https --only et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor`
exits 0. **Recoverability:** `data-2026-10-27-q128.json` was not edited, `data.json`'s `waterfall.v1` still
carries Q128's four steps, its 4.337 pJ rail and its 3.837 pJ remainder, and every new span sits inside the
script's `if (D.q129)` guard, so the 27 October page still rebuilds from either file. The private build
`et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor-private` was **not** touched. 🛑 The host's automated classifier flagged the
public space 4 s after the second deploy (`flag_reasons llm:S9`); anonymous access is unaffected (the viewer
answers 200, the raw host serves all 254,376 B, `check-mirror` exits 0), `spacesheep list --json` carries no
flag field, and there is nothing to do on the content — recorded for whoever owns the space.
**A third deploy followed the same day from another lane** (`989d91ee`, which lets a private box name any
element of the page as its anchor), so the two versions above are the record of the Q129 rebuilds and not
necessarily the live version: [MIRROR.md](../reports/MIRROR.md) and `check-mirror.py` are the authority, and they
read 1 of 1 equal when this entry was written (28 Oct 2026).

**Visibility.** Every space the hub links is public (Q40); the one exception, the David Kanter notes, is private and
no longer linked (below). After any deploy, compare `spacesheep list` with the record.

**The current record (since 2026-09-25)** of every page, its space uuid, its repo file, its visibility, its final
build command and its deploy form is [`../reports/MIRROR.md`](../reports/MIRROR.md), and
`python3 scripts/check-mirror.py` checks that each live page equals its file. This file keeps the history: what
each version changed, the reviews, the tools and the commits. Its visibility columns are dated snapshots.

---

**After 24 September.** Most rows above stop at 24 September; A2 carries its later entries, and A13 and A14 their
data's. Each page's own Versions line (at the end of its method section) gives one line per date, with the corrections
that changed a claim, and links the history of its source file, which has every wording (on 28 September the notes
were shortened to that; the longer ones are in the pages at `e659a5f`). The on-chip communication page has no A-number
here: its row is in "Other spaces the hub links" below. In brief, for every report in the table:

- **25 September (version 3):** each page checked claim by claim against aifoundry2 and aifoundry3 under the
  version-3 standard (`0399048`: 57 claims dropped or weakened, one-card results labelled; the record is
  `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/`).
- **26 September (version 4):** the three-card check's values, with aifoundry1's card 1, beside every claim it tested.
  The energy manual's catalogue, rings, levels, relay and tensor rows are the three cards' (A15); Horace §10 is
  rebuilt on three cards, with post-data note C2's two readings of the launch temperature (A4, A5); the DVFS page sets
  the idle law against the cooling cycles and no longer says busy leakage exceeds 30% (A11); memory anatomy, heat per
  millimetre, power and temperature, and ridge points take their three-card figures (A1, A17, A3, A19).
- **27 September:** the fixes from the review of 26 September (`docs/reports/TODO.md`), the chart pass and the
  collapsible depth (sections below), and the DVFS verdict table's row 6 cut (A11: five claims). E48's gathers and
  scatters went into the energy manual (§3.1's last table, §4.3's gather patterns, the new §4.4, with the rail split
  moved to §4.5, and six rows of §6; A15), into memory hierarchy (version 5, "Irregular access: gathers and scatters"),
  into influence functions (S3, section 3) and into the hub's chart of events.
- **28 September (the superseded energies):** the pages the hub tagged "baseline; energies superseded" now carry the
  current energies themselves, each first run (18 or 19 September) kept as one "first measured" note. On-chip
  communication: the energy chart's middle panel, its table and its notes give the rings' watts over idle and pJ/B
  from the energy manual's three-card re-run only (E43, 26 September; the first runs' hollow dots, ticks, fit and
  columns removed); `workloads/nocbench/analyze.py` now embeds `reruns.over_idle_w`, `reruns.spin_over_idle_w` and
  `reruns.first_vs_rerun`, re-reduced from the same V3-RL passes. Matmul efficiency: the lede, the KPIs, the
  efficiency explorer (now per card, from `v3`), the A100 table and Results are the three-card check's (E37), the
  per-card chart without the first run's row, the power chart marked as the first run's (it shows the method);
  `scripts/mmbench-report-data.py` embeds each card's cycles per op and prints the three-card summary the tables quote.
  Memory anatomy (A1): §6, the explorer in §1 and the DRAM figure at the top carry the energy manual's three-card
  values (E43's levels, E46's catalogue, 26 September) in place of E1's 19 September runs. §6 gives every level on
  each card and splits each way of reading a level (L1 hits, the own scratchpad, the L3 through the mesh, another
  shire's scratchpad 1, 3 and 6 hops away, DRAM) between the rails, per card and with zeros or random data, in a new
  chart and two tables. Its bullets quote the manual's hop fit, the unmetered fit and the DRAM row test. The
  regulators' delivery loss is the three-card fit's 10–19% by card (was 18–20%). The first runs are one "First
  measured" note (the host log against the rail trace, 14–25% in 12 runs). `workloads/memprobe/build_report.py` reads
  all of it from `manual.json` (its docstring lists the fields) and stops if the data no longer bear out the prose's
  qualitative claims. Memory hierarchy: its energies were already the three cards'; the method note and the Related
  link no longer call the 18 September energies superseded, and they stay as one note (board power against a ~27.0 W
  median idle, the governor free). Why low power (A5): its link to Memory hierarchy says that page's energies are the
  manual's. The hub's reading-path tags say where the energies now come from (memory anatomy's too), and its session
  notes (E1, E33, E34 and the matmul session) that the pages carry them.
  Later that day, Sparse compute: every power and energy figure is the three-card check's. The "Later measurements" box
  (the Horace experiment's 22 September session on aifoundry3) became §1's paragraph on operand values with a new chart,
  the same loop on all zeros, all ones and random normal data on each card (V3-ABL-A, E38, as the energy manual's §3.2)
  beside the page's own −3…3 configurations (V3-ABL-B, E39); the power chart lost its first-run view and the 22 September
  toggle, the layer table and explorer the first run's rows, and the first run (18 September, no temperature control) is
  one "first measured" note in the method; aifoundry1 card 1's saving at the same die temperature reads 92–96% on
  V3-ABL-B's registered metric (it said 91–96%, what the runs' dropout-rule metric gives). `workloads/sparsity/analyze.py` embeds
  `v3.operands` (from `results/abla.runs.json`) and each configuration's `at_die_w`, prints the values the text quotes
  (`page_summary()`), and no longer takes `--later`. The hub tags the page "18 Sep baseline; power from three cards".
  Memory levels: `l1.e-19sep` (now `l1.e-rails`), `l2.e.rails` and `l3.energy-per-load` give the per-card rail splits of
  Memory anatomy §6 (an L1 hit, the own scratchpad's read of the L2's arrays, an L3 read through the mesh, a mesh hop per
  line) in place of E1's 19 September runs, two of them citing lines of `workloads/memprobe/report_template.html` that no
  longer held those numbers; `build_facts.py` computes them from `manual.json` and stops if the data stop bearing out
  their qualitative claims, and `l3.wire-energy`, `l2.e.shire`, the L2 load hit's step 9 and the L3 load hit's steps 2
  and 8 follow, as do the L2 and L3 energy panels, the hop diagram and the reply step. The energy manual (A15): §5's ring
  note, in the markdown (`render_energy_manual.py`) and on the page, says On-chip communication shows the manual's values
  and keeps the 18 September pair as one note, and gives the s ↔ s+16 row's 13.27 pJ/B, not that run's 14.3. Power and
  temperature (A3): its Related link to Memory anatomy describes §6 as it now is. In `docs/findings/`, 13's A100 fp32
  lead is the matmul page's 2.8–3.9× by card (was 3.4×), and 15 points to Memory anatomy §6 for E1's current values.

### A4's versions

A4 was written in five versions as the question got sharper; a sixth added the second card's 7 s runs without
rewriting the rest. The file name never changed, so **only the latest is on disk**; the earlier ones survive in git
history and in the space's version list.

| Version | Commit | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `df89c94` | E7: the effect exists, with a fitted temperature correction |
| 2 | `04746a3` | E8: every run started at 80 °C |
| 3 | `f2b8776` | E9–E11: strict control, heating per FLOP, the RTL activity model, the cool-die speed effect, the first GIFs |
| 4 | `07f7d04` | E12–E16: ten-minute runs, the flips-to-temperature model, structured matrices priced before they ran |
| 5 | `75bb061` | E17: fit and prediction separated, held-out validation |
| 6 | `9df563e` | an addition, not a rewrite: E20's 7 s runs on aifoundry3 (the model transfers up to one scale factor); equations rendered as SVG |

Later revisions changed the page without rewriting it: the version-3 first pass (`0399048`), the visualization pass
(`3522c21`), the three cards' results in section 10 and beside the claims the check re-measured (`a852f4b`), the chart
of seconds to 90 °C (`347f232`) and the collapsible sections (`51720f3`).

### Rebuilding the standalone pages

Each command rewrites only its page's embedded JSON and is idempotent (a second run changes nothing; checked on
2026-09-25). After any of them, `python3 scripts/paste-chartkit.py PAGE` refreshes the chart toolkit block.

| Page | Command (from the repository root) |
|---|---|
| Matmul efficiency, and the test drive's ladder | `python3 scripts/mmbench-report-data.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-aifoundry2 --manual docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/manual.json --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-matmul-efficiency.html --ladder docs/report/index.html`. `--manual` adds the efficiency explorer's data; `--v3` (the default, the three-card check's `mmb.json`) embeds every energy the page quotes since 28 September and prints the three-card summary its tables quote; `--ladder` writes the test drive's `ladder-data`, the FOSDEM rungs parsed from `docs/et-soc1-notes.md`, and stops if that table changes shape |
| Sparse compute | `python3 workloads/sparsity/analyze.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-sparsity-aifoundry3 --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-sparsity.html` (since 26 September `--claims-v3` defaults to `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3` and embeds the three cards' TensorLoad rows and V3-ABL-B configurations as `v3`; `--claims-v3 none` leaves them out; since 28 September it also embeds V3-ABL-A's runs of the same loop on all zeros, all ones and random normal data as `v3.operands`, every power figure the page shows is the check's, and it ends by printing the values the text quotes; until then `--later docs/reports/data/2026-09-22-horace-aifoundry3/horace3.json` added the 22 September runs) |
| Memory hierarchy | `python3 workloads/memhier/analyze.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2 --v3 docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-memory-hierarchy.html` (E33; `--v3` embeds each card's V3-LAT chases, the passes `results/lat.json` keeps; `--reruns` defaults to the energy manual's `reruns.json`) |
| On-chip communication | `python3 workloads/nocbench/analyze.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-nocbench-aifoundry2 --memhier docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2 --search --v3 docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3 --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-on-chip-communication.html` (E34; about 30 s with `--search`; `--v3` takes the check's directory, not its `raw/`, and embeds each card's LAT-N1, N2 and N4 passes; `--reruns` defaults to the energy manual's `reruns.json`, and since 28 September the rings' watts over idle and the spin are re-reduced from the passes it names) |
| Ridge points (A19) | `python3 scripts/ridge-points.py --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-ridge-points.html` (A19 gives its input chain; since 26 September its cards are every card the energy manual carries in the entries it uses) |
| Memory anatomy (A1) | E1's three commands, with the three cards' `cards.json` first ("Rebuilding the version-3 data"); `build_report.py` reads it by default (`--v3`) and copies the toolkit from its template; since 28 September every energy on the page is read from `manual.json` (`reruns.levels_pj_per_byte`, `reruns.levels_by_contents_pj_per_byte`, `catalogue.combined`, each card's `catalogue.cards.<card>.summary` and `.wire`, `unmetered.<card>.coef`, `v3.catalogue.dram_rows`), and `build_report.py` stops if those data stop bearing out §6's qualitative claims |
| Spatial temperature brief | `python3 tools/ettelem/host_temp_fields.py` prints the two constants it embeds; `--check docs/reports/2026-09-22-et-soc1-spatial-temperature-brief.html` tests them |

### Rebuilding the version-3 data (26 September)

The version-3 check (E35–E47, three cards) feeds most pages through options on their usual generators. The order: the
check's own reduction first, then the page data; the energy data (A16) read the hot line's and the relay's files, so
rebuild those two before A16's chain. From the repository root:
```
D=docs/reports/data; V3=$D/2026-09-25-claims-v3; R=$V3/raw
# the check: collect every card's blocks (run from aifoundry2's checkout; smoke blocks left out; committed as $R),
# then every reducer. reduce_all.sh runs three exporters first (AMENDMENTS.md, implementation note of 26 Sep):
# ablb/export_mmb_values.py (ABLB-2b's kernel clause), rl/export_flop.py (RL-X4) and rl/export_low_edge.py (RL-f),
# into <out>/inputs/; a reducer whose input could not be built runs without it
tools/claims-v3/collect.sh <dir>
tools/claims-v3/reduce_all.sh $R $V3/results                    # <exp>.json and <exp>.log per experiment
# on-chip communication (E34 + each card's LAT-N1, N2, N4): --v3 takes the check's directory
python3 workloads/nocbench/analyze.py $D/2026-09-18-nocbench-aifoundry2 --memhier $D/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2 \
    --search --v3 $V3 --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-on-chip-communication.html
# memory hierarchy (E33 + each card's V3-LAT chases): --v3 takes raw/
python3 workloads/memhier/analyze.py $D/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2 --v3 $R \
    --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-memory-hierarchy.html
# memory anatomy's three cards (the V3-MEM passes the reducer kept); build_report.py reads this file by default
python3 workloads/memprobe/analyze.py --v3 $R --v3-passes $V3/results/mem.passes.json \
    --out $D/2026-09-26-memprobe-3cards/cards.json
# sparse compute: --claims-v3 defaults to $V3 (since 28 September without --later)
python3 workloads/sparsity/analyze.py $D/2026-09-18-sparsity-aifoundry3 --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-sparsity.html
# hot line (A13): the three cards' V3-LAT hot-line passes
python3 workloads/nocbench/analyze_hotline.py --v3 $R --power $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/power.json \
    --context $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/context.json --barrier $D/2026-09-18-nocbench-aifoundry2/barrier-chip1.jsonl \
    --stop-runs $D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry2-warm/hotline-pass*/runs.jsonl $D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry3/hotline-pass*/runs.jsonl \
    --out $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/hotline.json   # --stop-runs: the chart pass's context.window_stop_runs
# on-chip relay (A14): the three cards' V3-LAT relay sweeps, reduced to pass means
python3 workloads/onchip/analyze_onchip.py $R/aifoundry2/lat/p*/rl/sweep.jsonl $R/aifoundry3/lat/p*/rl/sweep.jsonl \
    $R/aifoundry1-c1/lat/p*/rl/sweep.jsonl --cards aifoundry2,aifoundry3,aifoundry1-c1 \
    --power $D/2026-09-22-onchip-aifoundry2 --out $D/2026-09-22-onchip-aifoundry2/onchip.json
# DVFS (A11): E19's command with --v3, then the 22 September cards block merged back in
H=$D/2026-09-21-horace-aifoundry2; A3=$D/2026-09-22-horace-aifoundry3; C=$D/2026-09-22-cards; R2=$D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry2
python3 tools/ettelem/analyze_dvfs.py --cold $H/cold1 $H/cold2 --wakeup $D/2026-09-22-dvfs-aifoundry2/wakeup \
    --idle $D/2026-09-22-dvfs-aifoundry2/idle_20h.jsonl.gz --since $H/long2/runs.jsonl.gz \
    --model $H/model.json --ablation $H/ablation.json --sptrace $C/sptrace-aifoundry3.bin \
    --cool-passes $R2/hotline-pass2 $R2/hotline-pass3 $R2/hotline-pass4 $R2/relay-pass1 $R2/relay-pass2 $R2/relay-pass4 \
    --idle-sessions $H $D/2026-09-2[234]-* --v3 $V3 --out $D/2026-09-22-dvfs-aifoundry2/dvfs.json
python3 tools/ettelem/build_cards_data.py --cards $A3/cards.json --transfer $A3/transfer.json \
    --leak $A3/leakage_crosscard.json --config $C/config.json --driver $C/driver_config.json \
    --sptrace $C/sptrace-aifoundry3.bin --out $C/cards-report.json --merge $D/2026-09-22-dvfs-aifoundry2/dvfs.json
# Horace (A4) section 10 and why-low-power (A5): the two --v3 lines at the end of tools/ettelem/finish_horace.sh
python3 tools/ettelem/build_cards_data.py --cards $A3/cards.json --transfer $A3/transfer.json \
    --leak $A3/leakage_crosscard.json --config $C/config.json --driver $C/driver_config.json \
    --sptrace $C/sptrace-aifoundry3.bin --v3 $V3/results --model $H/model.json --toggles-all $H/toggles_all.json \
    --out $H/cards-v3.json --merge $H/report.json
python3 tools/ettelem/build_lowpower_report_data.py --ablation $H/ablation.json --model $H/model.json \
    --toggles $H/toggles.json --vf $H/vf.json --second-card $A3/horace3.json --v3 $V3/results --out $H/lowpower-report.json
# heat per millimetre (A17): the check's six wire passes per card (wire3.json), then the report data from both runs
python3 workloads/enercat/analyze_wire_v3.py $R --out $D/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire3.json --pitch-x-mm 3.73 --pitch-y-mm 3.70
python3 tools/ettelem/build_wire_report.py --wire $D/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire.json \
    --wire3 $D/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire3.json --out $D/2026-09-24-wire-energy/report.json
# the energy data: A16's chain (catfull/reduce.py --catalogue-out, analyze_reruns.py --v3-rl, build_energy_manual.py),
# then the pages that read manual.json or reruns.json (the energy manual, the hub via sync_hub_data.py, memory
# anatomy, memory hierarchy, on-chip communication, ridge points, matmul efficiency)
```
Then build each page as MIRROR.md says. For post-data note C2 (each run at the die temperature of its launch, which
the whole-degree reading gives two ways: the reading itself, or 0.96 °C above it for a launch on a downward step of the
reading, as the references' were) `build_cards_data.py --v3` writes `at_launch` and `at_launch_step`,
`build_lowpower_report_data.py --v3` writes `dyn_at_launch` and `dyn_at_launch_step` per configuration and
`at_launch_offset_w` and `at_launch_step_offset_w` per card, and `build_energy_manual.py` writes `tensor.launch_offset`
with its `*_step` fields; the pages give each at-launch value as the range over the two readings. On 2026-09-26 every command above after the two
`tools/claims-v3` lines, and every step of A16's chain up to the renders, was run into a scratch directory and
reproduced its file in the tree byte for byte (for `--embed`, a copy of the page); `sync_hub_data.py --check` passed;
and `reduce_all.sh` over the committed `raw/` reproduced every value in `results/` and `results/inputs/` (only the
recorded paths, the timestamps and the name of RL-f's 23 September catalogue source differ). `results/pagemap.json` and `pagemap.md` (every page
claim with the items that test it) were written by a script that is not in the tree.

## Collapsible depth (27 September)

The owner found the pages too long to scroll ("the important things should show, and more detail hidden under
collapsibles"). The toolkit gained `details.more` (style in `report.template.html`'s chartkit CSS) and `CK.reveal`,
which opens every `<details>` around the target of an address or in-page link; printing opens them all. Seven agents
then folded method, derivations, caveats, version histories, long tables and "Reproduce" blocks on 20 pages (the L2
pointer brief left as it was), keeping each section's finding and main chart in view: visible text fell to 42–60%
of before (the hub 76k to 35k characters, Horace 68k to 30k). Nothing was deleted or reworded: each page, rendered
with every section open, still contains every line of its old text, and passes `check_page.sh` at 1280 and 390 px
in light and dark. The two aifoundry1 pages carry their own copy of the style and `reveal()`. New pages should keep
to this: a section's finding and main chart visible, the rest in `details.more` with a plain label.

## A12 — Math rendered at build time

`scripts/tex2svg.js` turns the TeX in a report body into standalone SVG, called from
`scripts/build-report.py`: display math between `$$`, inline math between `\(` and `\)`, anything inside
`<pre>` or `<code>` left alone. It needs mathjax-full, pinned to 3.2.1 in the repo's `package.json` and
`package-lock.json`: run `npm ci` once at the repo root (3.2.2 gives byte-identical SVGs). In a git worktree without
its own `node_modules`, set `NODE_PATH` to a checkout's.

**Why not the usual runtime MathJax:** the first attempt loaded MathJax from `cdn.jsdelivr.net`, and the
publishing host blocks external scripts in viewers with a content-security policy — the deploy says so. Every
equation would have shown as raw TeX to every reader. Pre-rendering also means the files stay self-contained
and the math is identical in every browser. MathJax colours its SVG with `currentColor`, so dark mode works
with no extra rule.

**The chart toolkit (added 2026-09-25).** `docs/reports/sources/chartkit.js` is one small shared library, no
dependencies, defining the global `CK`: SVG helpers, charts sized to their container (one unit is one CSS pixel, so
text stays legible on a phone), scales and axes, tooltips that open on hover, focus and tap, keyboard stepping over
marks, legends that toggle series, segmented controls, labelled sliders, a live readout, linked views, stacked
tables for narrow screens, and colours taken only from the page's theme tokens so dark mode keeps its contrast.
`scripts/build-report.py` inlines it into `report.template.html` at `__CHARTKIT__`, just before the page script, and
its CSS sits in the template between `chartkit:css:begin` and `chartkit:css:end`. Standalone pages get the same JS,
the CSS and an alias block mapping their own token names onto the kit's through `scripts/paste-chartkit.py PAGE`
(between `<!-- chartkit:begin -->` and `<!-- chartkit:end -->`; `--check` reports drift). Its API is in PLAN2 §2.5
(`docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-report-review/PLAN2.md`). Added on 26 September: the card registry (`CK.cards`,
`CK.card`, `CK.cardsIn`, `CK.cardSeg`, `CK.pick`, `CK.cardMark`, `CK.cardLegend`: one colour, mark and label per
card, cards taken from the data so a new card appears when its data does) and `CK.sortTable` (sortable, filterable
tables); on 27 September `CK.reveal` and the `details.more` style ("Collapsible depth", above).

Each has a `.png` poster beside it in `docs/reports/`. They are generated by `tools/ettelem/make_heating_gif.py` and

They live in A4's space, so **A4 must always be deployed as a folder** (`index.html` plus the three GIFs). On
2026-09-24 a single-file redeploy of A4 dropped the GIFs and broke their public addresses; they were restored the same
day by redeploying the folder. The posters stay in the repository and are not deployed: no page references them, and
on 2026-09-25 `horace-heating.png` answered 404 at the space's address while the three GIFs answered 200 and equalled
their repo files.

## Tools (all under `tools/ettelem/` unless noted)

| Tool | What it does |
|---|---|
| `ettelem` (C++, `tools/ettelem/ettelem.cpp`) | Telemetry client on `libDM.so`. `sample` emits JSON lines at up to 45 Hz: board power, the PMIC's per-rail running averages (as the service processor forwards them) and voltages, die and PMIC temperatures, clocks. `config` reads the governor's static inputs: the static TDP level (on aifoundry3 set to 0 at every boot by a service, not flashed), the software temperature threshold and the power state (E21). Also `loglevel` and `sptrace`. **Holds the management node open**, which is single-opener |
| `etcfg` (C, `tools/etcfg/etcfg.c`) | The driver's `ETSOC1_IOCTL_GET_DEVICE_CONFIGURATION` as JSON: TDP, boot clock, shire mask, cache sizes. Read-only, no management node, so it works on a machine whose `libDM.so` refuses the card (E21) |
| `compare_cards.py` | Two cards' strict sessions side by side, with the model's predicted switching power for each pattern. Fits nothing (E20) |
| `run_hotline_power.sh`, `analyze_hotline_power.py` | Board power and rails while one atomic line is contended, against the same work spread over 32 (E23) |
| `workloads/nocbench --test hotline` | Many-to-one contention on one global atomic word: per-shire shares in a common window, the host shire's own traffic, the requester and pacing sweeps (E22, E23) |
| `workloads/nocbench/run_hotline.sh`, `analyze_hotline.py` | The 42-configuration sweep and its analysis |
| `workloads/onchip` | `--test probe`: can one shire read what another wrote into its scratchpad, and by which write path (E24). `--test relay`: the same multi-stage computation with its intermediate in DRAM, in the shire's own scratchpad, or in the next shire's (E25). Every run verifies its own output |
| `workloads/onchip/run_onchip.sh`, `analyze_onchip.py` | The 88-configuration sweep and its analysis |
| `run_onchip_power.sh` | Board power per medium, one twelve-second burst each, sized so the card is busy 83% of the time |
| `workloads/enercat` | The instruction and byte energy catalogue: one pattern flat out on every hart, 56 configurations, `run_enercat.sh` and `analyze_enercat.py` (E26) |
| `build_energy_manual.py`, `render_energy_manual.py`, `render_catalogue.py` | Assemble every table of the manual from its data file into one JSON, and render the markdown from it, so no number on the page is typed by hand |
| `workloads/enercat/gen_ops.py` | Generates the kernel's 174 instruction cases and the host's mode table from one instruction list (E27) |
| `workloads/enercat/run_catalogue.py`, `analyze_catalogue.py` | The 386-configuration, three-pass shuffled catalogue and its reduction: per-configuration statistics, the wire fit, the rail split, the SRAM leakage (E27, E28) |
| `ettelem sample --reset-ms` | Resets the service processor's rail statistics on a schedule (and sends the PMIC its stats reset) and tags each sample with its window; the min and max then cover the window. E41 used it on three cards (26 September): the reset also restarts the PMIC's running average, so one second after a burst a rail has fallen 93% of the way (0.45–0.68 predicted) |
| `build_cards_data.py` | Assembles the three-machine block and merges it into both reports' data (E20, E21). `--v3 <results> --model --toggles-all` (2026-09-26): the Horace page's section 10 on the version-3 check's three cards, merged into `report.json` only (the 22 September block kept as `history`), with `at_launch` and `at_launch_step` for post-data note C2 |
| `run_horace_strict.sh` | The strict-start protocol, shuffled blocks, 7 s runs (E9) |
| `run_horace_cold.sh` | Runs from a cool die, for the governor (E10) |
| `run_horace_long.sh` | Long runs with a temperature cap and a watchdog (E12, E16) |
| `run_ablation.sh` + `ablation.cfg` | Strict-start runs of arbitrary `sparsity_host` configurations (E15) |
| `run_vf_cold.sh` | A careful voltage/frequency comparison. **Written, never run** |
| `make_tiles.py` | Structured 16×16 operand pairs (E13) |
| `analyze_horace_strict.py`, `analyze_horace_long.py`, `analyze_horace_cold.py`, `analyze_ablation.py` | Per-run and per-pattern analysis |
| `flip_thermal_model.py` | Fits the flips → power → temperature model (E17) |
| `validate_flip_model.py` | Tests a **frozen** model on data it was not fitted to; fits nothing (E17) |
| `predict_heat.py` | Prices a custom workload from its operand tiles: flips, watts, heating curve, time to a cap, sustainable duty cycle |
| `compact_telemetry.py` | Shrinks a telemetry log ~70× for storage in the repo |
| `build_vf.py` (2026-09-25) | The 600 against 800 MHz operating points of the cool starts, `vf.json`, with every window stated in its `rules` field (the file was hand-entered before; the random-data peak is 87.8 W) |
| `transfer_cards.py` (2026-09-25) | How the aifoundry2 flip model and idle law transfer to aifoundry3: `transfer.json` and `leakage_crosscard.json`, reproducing the first-published fields byte for byte and adding the worst rms, the worst single error, the sample-weighted offset and the offset under the model's own idle rule (E20) |
| `finish_horace.sh` | Rebuilds the Horace line from the stored raw data: every analysis of E9–E17, the model, the validations, `build_vf.py`, the GIFs, the E20 second-card block (aifoundry3's `analyze_horace_strict.py`, `compare_cards.py`, `transfer_cards.py`, then `build_cards_data.py`), and the Horace and why-low-power pages. Since 2026-09-25 it no longer runs the unused non-causal `model2` evaluation; `report.json` no longer carries `structured_long` or `power_model.before`, and `lowpower-report.json` gains `model.step_open` and `model.loop_gain_at_80`. Since 2026-09-26 it ends with the version-3 lines (`build_cards_data.py --v3`, `build_lowpower_report_data.py --v3`) before the two page builds |
| `summarize_power_session.py` (2026-09-25) | Reduces the 20 September load step (E5, E6) to the `summary.json` A3 reads: the 1 s series (reproducing the committed one exactly), the launch gaps and the fast samples from the 10 Hz stream, the load log, the voltage map's repeat check, and the later measurements A3 compares with, each with its source |
| `parse_sptrace_voltage.py` (2026-09-25) | The per-shire voltage map from an SP trace dump; on the recovered `raw/sp1.bin` it reproduces `per-shire-voltage-idle.json` byte for byte (`--self-test`, `--compare`) |
| `host_temp_fields.py` (2026-09-25) | The temperature fields the host received, as change points, on both cards; prints the constants the spatial temperature brief embeds, and `--check PAGE` tests them |
| `rtl-sim/fma_toggle/` | The Verilator bench that counts switching activity (E11, E13) |
| `rtl-sim/pmu_carry/` | The Verilator bench that reproduces the counter bug (E2) |
| `analyze_dvfs.py` | Classifies every governor transition against the firmware's thresholds (unattributed where neither fired on the sampled values), computes the long idle's duration from timestamps (`--since`), counts aifoundry3's governor log events (`--sptrace`), and assembles the DVFS report's data (fits nothing). Since 2026-09-25 it keeps an existing `cards` block when it rewrites `dvfs.json`, no longer corrects the wake-up latencies' late carry a second time, takes the operating points' voltages from telemetry, and writes `leak_fraction.idle_80c_law` (65%) beside the measured-denominator `idle_80c` (64%); the full command is under E19. `--v3 docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3` (2026-09-26) adds the check's per-card readouts (`v3`) and its idle cooling cycles ("Rebuilding the version-3 data") |
| `workloads/memprobe/gen_ops.py wakeup` | The array wake-up probe (E18) |
| `tools/claims-v3/` (2026-09-25) | The measurement framework of E35–E48: `lib.sh` (the lab rules in code: no start while anyone else uses the card, `timeout 10`, the card lock, code hashes, `V3_DRY=1`), `queue.sh` and `schedule-<card>.txt`, one directory per experiment (`block.sh`, `reduce.py`, a README), `collect.sh` and `reduce_all.sh`; AGENT.md §7 |
| `workloads/enercat` with `-DENERCAT_GS=ON` (2026-09-25) | Gathers, scatters and packed atomics: `gs_catalogue.py` (the configurations), `gen_gs.py` (tables and kernel cases), `analyze_gs.py` (the analysis), run by `tools/claims-v3/gs/` (E48) |
| `tools/g3log-race/race.cpp` (2026-09-25) | Reproduces the runtime's log-level race without a card: resets g3log's levels, releases four threads that each make the first `g3::logLevel()` call, and counts the trials that corrupt the level map, with and without registering the level first (E49) |
| `tools/claims-v3/nv/` (2026-09-28) | NV (E54), the mesh rail's voltage step: `block.sh` (development passes 1–99 on aifoundry3, validation 101–199 on aifoundry2), its own `run-queue.sh`, `nvlib.py`, `reduce.py` (with `--self-test` and `--monte-carlo`), `selftest.sh` (40 dry scenarios with stand-ins for every device tool), `freeze.sh`; only 485, 540 and 600 mV can be sent, only on BL2 0.19.0 or later, and a restore guardian returns the rail to 485 mV on every exit path. No voltage has been written yet |
| `tools/claims-v3/pcie2/`, `workloads/pciebench` `--test conc` and `--test touch` (2026-09-28) | pcie2 (E55), hub rungs 34 and 35: host-to-card DMA commands in flight in one stream and in two, and where a host write lands (the touch kernel times the first load of each line the host has just written); `PREREG.md`, a reducer, a dry test. `workloads/pciebench/run_pcie.sh` gained `timeout 10` on its samples, the card lock released between sub-tests, never card 0, and `CARDS` from the environment |
| `tools/claims-v3/nocr/`, `workloads/nocroute` (2026-09-28) | nocr (E56), hub rungs 31 and 32: an ESR call's cost per mesh hop over caller-target pairs, the place of the master shire, and the routing order of read replies and write requests; `meshmap.py` draws the mesh, `reduce.py` holds the frozen rules |
| `tools/claims-v3/memp2/`, `workloads/memprobe` `MEMPROBE_EXT` (`gen_ops2.py`, built as `build/memprobe2`) (2026-09-28) | memp2 (E57), hub rungs 33, 36 and 43 and energy-manual-102: DRAM bank and row bits, the refresh domain, whether the L2 keeps TensorLoad lines, the 128 B per cycle cap, stride-256 energy; `sysemu.sh` ran the new kernel paths in `sys_emu` before any card time (18 runs, PASS) |
| `tools/claims-v3/tau/`, `tools/ettelem/deconv.py` (2026-09-28) | tau (E58), hub rung 4: each rail's averaging time constant, fitted to burst edges, and `deconv.py`, which inverts the first-order filter to recover square bursts from the rail readings; `calibrate.py`, `offline.py` (the fit on stored data), stand-ins for dry runs |
| `tools/lab/et-lab-health` rev 3, `tools/lab/et-reset` (2026-09-28) | The lab tools' third revision: the health check counts logins through logind, counts the journal's boots correctly and runs safely from a timer; `et-reset` is a checked management reset of one card. Both written and dry-tested, **not installed** ([`tools/lab/README.md`](../../tools/lab/README.md)) |
| `tools/timeline/` (2026-09-28) | The session timeline's extraction, privacy scan and build (A20) |
| `tools/timeline/build_transistor_timeline.py` (2026-10-26) | The transistor-heat timeline's build and privacy scan, from curated JSON (A23) |
| `workloads/sparsity/host/main.cpp` | Extended in this work with `--values` (operand patterns, including `file:<path>` for custom tiles), `--dump-tiles`, `--stop-file` and fp16/int8 value fills |

## A16 — The rerun and attribution tools (2026-09-23)

- `tools/ettelem/analyze_reruns.py`: reduces every rerun pass with bracketing idle and the leakage correction,
  drops bursts whose clock left 600 MHz or whose sampler was starved, pools per card and over cards.
- `tools/ettelem/run_reruns_warm.sh`, `run_rl_warm_a2.sh`: passes preceded by heating the die past 76 °C.
- `tools/ettelem/run_rings_levels_power.sh`: the nocbench rings and memhier levels sampled by ettelem.
- `run_onchip_power.sh`, `run_hotline_power.sh`: the sampler now retries until it produces a line.
- `tools/ettelem/render_energy_manual.py`, `render_catalogue.py`, `build_energy_manual.py`: bars everywhere,
  the reruns and `unmetered_fit.json` carried into `manual.json`; `render_catalogue.py` writes the attribution
  and droop sections of 04a.
- `docs/reports/sources/limits-of-observability.{body.html,data.json,script.js,meta.json}`: the report now builds
  with the shared `scripts/build-report.py` like every other; the old `scripts/build-observability-report.py` was removed in `8084699`.
  Since 2026-09-25 `tools/ettelem/sync_hub_data.py` writes the data file's computed blocks (`power.idle_73c`,
  `power.fit`, `power.per_config`, `power.droop`, `power.rail_filter`, `power.sampler`, `power.idle_unsensed`,
  `power.checks`, `energy_events`, `claims_status`) from `dvfs.json`, `unmetered_fit.json`, `catalogue.json`, the
  energy data and the version-3 check (`plan3.json.gz`, `results/`, the V3-CATFULL telemetry), so no hub number is
  copied by hand: `power.checks` holds the fit's robust errors and per-pass coefficients and the droop calibration
  on each card's own telemetry, and `claims_status` the claims check's verdicts per page before and after the
  campaign (its `CLAIM_SERIES`), for §1's scoreboard. Run it before `build-report.py` (`--only <blocks>` rewrites
  some and keeps the rest); `--check` exits 1 if the data file is stale. The hub's text blocks stay hand-kept in the
  data file.
- `tools/ettelem/fit_unmetered.py` (added 2026-09-24): the unmetered-power attribution and the DDR droop fit of E30,
  from `catalogue.json` and the aifoundry2 catalogue telemetry. `unmetered_fit.json` was first computed inline, and
  the script reproduced its attribution exactly but not its droop block (0.87 against 0.84 mV/W; the inline windows
  were not recorded). Since 2026-09-25 the script writes the file
  (`python3 tools/ettelem/fit_unmetered.py --out docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/unmetered_fit.json
  --overwrite`): the attribution is unchanged (to floating-point rounding, about 1e-15), the droop block is the script's (0.87 mV per off-rail DRAM watt,
  0.029 per watt of anything else, rms 0.37 mV, minion IR drop 0.070 mV/W), and it adds each configuration's row and
  the refit with the line reads of stores through the L1 counted, for comparison.
- `workloads/enercat/analyze_catalogue.py` (2026-09-25): also records the sampler's latency per burst and per
  configuration (`sampler_median_ms`, `sampler_max_ms`) and measures the rails' filter (`rail_filter`); every
  earlier value in `catalogue.json` is unchanged.
- `tools/ettelem/analyze_reruns.py` (2026-09-25): also pools the hot line's watts over idle (`hotline_over_idle_w`),
  taking the first session from its `power.json`.

**Rebuilding the energy data, in order** (no card; each step reads the files the one before writes):
```
D=docs/reports/data; M=$D/2026-09-23-energy-manual
# since 26 Sep 2026: the version-3 full catalogue on three cards (V3-CATFULL), reduced with analyze_catalogue's own code
python3 tools/claims-v3/catfull/reduce.py --data $D/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw --out /tmp/catfull-verdicts.json \
    --catalogue-out $M/catalogue.json --cards aifoundry2,aifoundry3,aifoundry1-c1
python3 tools/ettelem/fit_unmetered.py --out $M/unmetered_fit.json --overwrite
python3 workloads/enercat/analyze_enercat.py $D/2026-09-23-enercat-aifoundry2 $D/2026-09-23-enercat-aifoundry3 \
    --out $M/enercat.json
python3 tools/ettelem/analyze_hotline_power.py $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2 --out $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/power.json
python3 workloads/nocbench/analyze_hotline.py --v3 $D/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw \
    --power $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/power.json --context $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/context.json \
    --barrier $D/2026-09-18-nocbench-aifoundry2/barrier-chip1.jsonl \
    --stop-runs $D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry2-warm/hotline-pass*/runs.jsonl $D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry3/hotline-pass*/runs.jsonl \
    --out $D/2026-09-22-hotline-aifoundry2/hotline.json
R=$D/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw      # the relay's pass means on three cards, which build_energy_manual.py also reads
python3 workloads/onchip/analyze_onchip.py $R/aifoundry2/lat/p*/rl/sweep.jsonl $R/aifoundry3/lat/p*/rl/sweep.jsonl \
    $R/aifoundry1-c1/lat/p*/rl/sweep.jsonl --cards aifoundry2,aifoundry3,aifoundry1-c1 \
    --power $D/2026-09-22-onchip-aifoundry2 --out $D/2026-09-22-onchip-aifoundry2/onchip.json
python3 tools/ettelem/analyze_reruns.py $D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry2-warm $D/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry3 \
    --v3-rl $D/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw --out $M/reruns.json
python3 tools/ettelem/build_energy_manual.py --out $M/manual.json
python3 tools/ettelem/render_energy_manual.py $M/manual.json docs/energy-manual/
python3 tools/ettelem/render_catalogue.py $M/catalogue.json docs/energy-manual/
python3 scripts/build-report.py energy-manual $M/manual.json docs/reports/2026-09-23-energy-manual.html
python3 tools/ettelem/sync_hub_data.py      # the hub's computed blocks, then build the hub:
python3 scripts/build-report.py limits-of-observability docs/reports/sources/limits-of-observability.data.json \
    docs/reports/2026-09-20-et-soc1-limits-of-observability.html
```
`build_energy_manual.py` reads the synchronisation table's latencies from the version-3 check's `results/lat.json`
(since 27 September: the in-shire barrier and the 32-minion allreduce from LAT-N1, the chip barrier with one minion
per shire and with all 1,024 from LAT-N4, every kept pass on three cards; the waiting energy uses the all-minion
passes' mean), and keeps `barrier-chip32.jsonl` (5,018 cycles, one run on aifoundry2, 18 September) as history.
`analyze_hotline.py --v3` copies LAT-N4's chip barrier from the `results/lat.json` beside the raw directory into
`hotline.json` (`context.barrier_chip_v3`) for the hot line's section 7. Since 26 September the hot
line's and the relay's steps are their version-3 forms ("Rebuilding the version-3 data"); `build_energy_manual.py`
reads both files (`hotline.json`, `onchip.json`).

Since 26 September 2026 (the version-3 check, three cards) the chain takes its catalogue from V3-CATFULL (the command
above; the 23 September catalogue stays as `$M/catalogue-23sep.json`, which `analyze_catalogue.py` on the three
`2026-09-23-catalogue-*` directories reproduces and the version-3 reducers read), the relay, rings and levels from
the V3-RL passes (`analyze_reruns.py --v3-rl`; the hot line keeps the 23 September reruns; a ring with no kept V3-RL
burst on any card, the s <-> s+16 one, keeps its 23 September passes), and the tensor rows, bars and awake loop
from V3-ABL-A (`results/abla.runs.json`, read by `build_energy_manual.py`); `build_energy_manual.py` also copies each
card's idle, the card-to-card tests and the meter's refresh from the check's results files into `manual.json` `v3`,
and fits each other card's own idle law (`rest.per_card`). `fit_unmetered.py` fits every card in `catalogue.json` and
reads aifoundry2's droop from its nine catfull blocks' telemetry (`catalogue_telemetry(card)`, and `droop()` takes a
list of files). `enercat.json` is unchanged (the 13-instruction first catalogue has no version-3 run).

## A18 — The wire tools (2026-09-24)

- `workloads/enercat/run_wire.py`: both runs of the heat-per-mm experiment (E31 `--set v1`, E32 `--set v2`): the
  fills, shuffled passes bracketed by idle, `marks.jsonl` for the prefill and heater windows, and a sampler that is
  retried, drained with `dev_mngt_service` on a failed start, and restarted if it stalls.
- `workloads/enercat/analyze_wire.py`: the per-burst reduction (board power with the leakage correction, the mesh
  rail), per-pass slopes against distance, the model s0 + a·2P(1−P) + b·P, the complement test, the link-disjoint
  flows, the axes, per mm; and the report's checks (link sharing from the recorded reader>target maps under XY and
  YX routing, readers, per-reader bandwidth, the four-hop point off the line, the exit step, the 256 B excess) and
  the model refitted without the leakage correction (`--no-leak-correction` for the whole reduction).
- `tools/ettelem/build_wire_report.py`: joins `wire.json` with the sourced inputs (die, pitch, NoC, literature, first
  principles) into `report.json`; `tools/ettelem/chain_wire_v2.sh` starts the second run when the first is done.
  Since 2026-09-25 it also reads `research/geometry/pitch.json` (`memshire_w_mm`), `workloads/nocbench/analyze.py`'s
  `MARTY`/`EMPTY` layout (`inputs.mesh_xy`) and the four raw `runs.jsonl.gz` files (the reader>target maps of the
  `wu`/`wsep` p0.5 configurations, for the page's link-sharing map); it resolves every path from its own location,
  so it runs from any directory, and stops if an input is missing.
- `workloads/enercat/analyze_wire_v3.py` (2026-09-26): the same reduction (`analyze_wire.analyze()`, unchanged) over
  the version-3 check's wire passes (`raw/<card>/wire/p<N>/`, the first six ok passes per card, bursts loaded with
  `tools/claims-v3/wire/reduce.py`'s drop rules), into `wire3.json`. `build_wire_report.py` now requires it
  (`--wire3`); the 24 September runs stay as `wire_sep24` for what only they measured (optional `--check`: the V3-WIRE
  verdicts).
- The commands behind the committed files, from the repository root (the first reproduces `wire.json` byte for byte;
  the pitches are the die geometry's x and y hop in mm):
  ```
  python3 workloads/enercat/analyze_wire.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire{,2}-aifoundry{2,3} \
      --out docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire.json --pitch-x-mm 3.73 --pitch-y-mm 3.70
  python3 workloads/enercat/analyze_wire_v3.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw \
      --out docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire3.json --pitch-x-mm 3.73 --pitch-y-mm 3.70
  python3 tools/ettelem/build_wire_report.py --wire docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire.json \
      --wire3 docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire-energy/wire3.json --out docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire-energy/report.json
  python3 scripts/build-report.py heat-per-mm docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-wire-energy/report.json \
      docs/reports/2026-09-24-heat-per-mm.html
  ```
- `workloads/enercat` host and kernel: `--pattern tstore_raw` (stores the host's bytes exactly) and `tstore_uniq`
  (every 512 B block unique, two regions per target), operand kinds `bern:P`, `alt:N`, `uq:P`, `frz`, options
  `--hop-axis`, `--pairs`, `--uniq-regions`, `--dump-slice`, the target map in every run record, and the guard that
  refuses a memory pattern with no slice.
- `tools/ettelem/ettelem.cpp`: `sample` finishes its request and exits on SIGTERM or SIGINT, so stopping it no longer
  poisons the management queue; `run_onchip_power.sh`, `run_hotline_power.sh` and `run_rings_levels_power.sh` drain
  the queue on a failed start.
- `docs/reports/sources/heat-per-mm.{body.html,script.js,meta.json}`: the report; every number in its sentences is
  computed from `report.json`.

## A19 — The ridge-point tool (computed 2026-09-18, published 2026-09-24)

- `scripts/ridge-points.py`: reads the raw data of the four 2026-09-18 reports (`docs/reports/data/*/runs.jsonl`;
  for the matmul, `2026-09-18-aifoundry2/runs.jsonl` rather than the `results.json` beside it) and their embedded
  JSON, checks the bandwidths against the 23 September reruns of the same probe on both cards (the `rl-pass*` runs the
  energy manual pools, not the older `levels-pass*`: within 0.3%), takes the energies per FLOP and per byte from the
  energy manual's data (`docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/manual.json`: the reruns, `tensor.bars`,
  `tensor.rows`, `awake`, `catalogue.combined` and `catalogue.cards` for the write rates and the spin loop), and prints
  every ridge point, the clock elasticity of each level, the limits that act like ridge points, the energy balance
  points (on random, all-ones and all-zero operands) and the A100 comparison. `--embed <html>` rewrites the report's
  `ridge-data` JSON; running it twice leaves the page byte for byte identical. Every spec constant in it (port and
  bank widths, LPDDR4X rates, PCIe, clocks) names its source in a comment. No card time.
- **The command chain** the page's Reproduce section points to. Its inputs rebuild from committed data (only the raw
  probe and matmul runs needed a card):
  ```
  python3 tools/claims-v3/catfull/reduce.py --data docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw \
      --out /tmp/catfull-verdicts.json --catalogue-out docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/catalogue.json \
      --cards aifoundry2,aifoundry3,aifoundry1-c1
  python3 tools/ettelem/analyze_reruns.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry2-warm \
      docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-reruns-aifoundry3 --v3-rl docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw \
      --out docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/reruns.json
  python3 tools/ettelem/build_energy_manual.py --out docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/manual.json
  python3 workloads/nocbench/analyze.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-nocbench-aifoundry2 \
      --memhier docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2 --search --v3 docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3 \
      --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-on-chip-communication.html
  python3 workloads/memhier/analyze.py docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2 \
      --v3 docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-memory-hierarchy.html
  python3 scripts/ridge-points.py --embed docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-ridge-points.html
  python3 scripts/paste-chartkit.py docs/reports/2026-09-18-et-soc1-ridge-points.html
  ```
  (`build_energy_manual.py` needs every input of the energy manual; A16 lists the full order.) On 2026-09-25 each step
  reproduced its committed file byte for byte, and a second `--embed` changed nothing. Since 26 September the chain
  takes the version-3 check's three cards (the catalogue from V3-CATFULL, the reruns' relay, rings and levels from
  V3-RL, both pages' `--v3`); on that day each step again reproduced its file in the tree ("Rebuilding the version-3
  data").

## A20 — The session timeline (published 27 September; page, data and tools in the repository since 28 September)

[A week with the ET-SoC-1: the session timeline](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-session-timeline)
(`b0669cbd-6132-4ac6-b35a-9b928a2ef926`, public): the session's week on one time axis, with the owner's requests as
summaries, the main agent and its subagents, the four cards, and every deploy and commit. Until 28 September its page
and tools lived outside the repository (MIRROR.md's check of 17:37 PDT warned about it); at the owner's request (Q65)
they were committed in `6bbf0f3` (28 Sep 21:52 PDT), refreshed to 21:15 PDT, and the live page redeployed. It was
refreshed again to 29 September, and on 1 October, for the owner's request Q83, to the snapshot of 1 Oct 02:26 PDT: 151
messages, 912 subagents in 65 workflow runs, 330 deploys and 200 commits from 19 September, with highlights to 52 (the
logger installed and the dashboard public, the driver race reported, a departed user's checkpoints deleted, the low
clock's answer, the memory levels' level tabs, the chip diagram's powers of ten). That refresh added the neighbor
session's lane (the session of 30 September that ran the link test which hung aifoundry1, with its messages as
summaries, its key events and the hosts down) and new gestures, after the reusable component's lessons
(spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/scrollable-session-timeline): vertical page scrolling stays native over the chart, a touch
or a wheel gesture keeps the axis it starts with, a sideways swipe pans with momentum, a pinch and Ctrl/⌘ + wheel zoom
about the fingers or the pointer, a tap shows a tooltip and a second tap on a flag zooms there, and frames are drawn as
one path per series while a gesture runs (`tools/timeline/README.md`, "Gestures").

- **Page:** `docs/reports/2026-09-27-session-timeline.html`, built by `scripts/build-report.py session-timeline` from
  `docs/reports/sources/session-timeline.*` and `docs/reports/data/2026-09-27-session-timeline/timeline.json` (the
  build line is in MIRROR.md, "How each page is built").
- **Data:** the eight extracts in `docs/reports/data/2026-09-27-session-timeline/` (`human.json`, `main_agent.json`,
  `agents.json`, `tokens_by_time.json`, `card_calls.json`, `cards.json`, `artifacts.json`, and since 1 October
  `neighbors.json`, the neighbor session's lane), from which any checkout
  rebuilds the page. The owner's messages appear as hand-written summaries, never their text.
- **Tools:** [`tools/timeline/`](../../tools/timeline/README.md). The extraction (`extract_main.py`,
  `extract_agents.py`, `tokens_by_time.py`, `extract_card_calls.py`, `build_cards.py`, `scan_spacesheep.py`,
  `extract_neighbors.py`, `build_artifacts.py`) reads the session's transcripts and the lab machines' queue logs, which are not in the
  repository, so only the owner's machine runs it; `paths.py` holds every path, set by environment variables. The build
  (`sanitize_extracts.py`, then `build_timeline_data.py`) needs only the committed extracts.
- **The privacy scan:** `sanitize_extracts.py` rewrites the extracts with the page's redactions from a local privacy
  table (never committed) and fails on anything AGENT.md §10 excludes (privilege words, access terms, addresses, keys,
  e-mail addresses, local paths, a space that MIRROR.md does not list as public); `build_timeline_data.py` refuses to
  write `timeline.json` while it fails.
- **Gesture checks:** `tools/timeline/gestures/` (`suite.mjs` and its helpers; Node, no packages) drive the page in the
  page checker's headless Chrome with synthesized touch and wheel input on two phones and a desktop. On the page of
  1 October: a vertical swipe over the chart scrolls the page 310 px and leaves the view, a 45° swipe scrolls the page,
  a sideways swipe pans and glides on, a pinch zooms, a tap on a flag pins its tooltip and a second tap zooms; on the
  desktop a vertical wheel scrolls the page, deltaX and Shift + wheel pan, Ctrl + wheel zooms about the pointer (22 s
  of drift); rAF p95 16.8 ms on the 4× throttled phone. Pass the page as an absolute path (the suite opens
  `file://` + the argument).

## A21 — Sparse parity on the ET-SoC-1 (29 September)

The page for Q66 and E59: noisy sparse parity solved on the card's 1,024 minions, against the host CPU's best method.

- **Page:** `docs/reports/2026-09-29-sparse-parity.html`, slug `et-soc1-sparse-parity`, space
  `a6212e3c-63f8-4971-a833-06457e2824da`: deployed private at 12:45 PDT on 29 September, made public at the owner's
  word that afternoon (Q67). On the hub it sits beside the influence-functions page, in §1's index under "Research
  and exploratory", with E59 in §7's sessions. [`../reports/MIRROR.md`](../reports/MIRROR.md) has its build command.
- **What it rests on:** E59, milestones M1–M5 on aifoundry3's card on 29 September (L1 0.131 s, L2 0.323 s and
  (256, 5) 1.52 s on 1,024 minions; 7.8–12× one core and 1.1–1.6× six tuned AVX-512 threads at P(loss) ≤ 10⁻⁴; board
  energy per solve with idle, measured on the one-stage scan, 5.0, 16.6 and 89 J: the board alone 2.5–7.7× below the
  CPU's assumed package energy at 125–251 W, 1.6–6.1× with the host that drives the card); the numbers are in
  [05-claims.md](05-claims.md), "Sparse parity on the ET-SoC-1".
- **Data:** `workloads/sparseparity/data/2026-09-29-aifoundry3-{card,card-m4,m5-energy}/` (the card),
  `workloads/sparseparity/data/2026-09-29-aifoundry3-sysemu*/` (the simulator), `workloads/sparseparity/cpu/data/`
  (the CPU baselines) and `workloads/sparseparity/proto/data/2026-09-28-aifoundry1/` (the toy prototypes).
- **Research:** `docs/research/sparse-parity/`: `literature.md` (SP1), `chip.md` (SP2), `DESIGN.md` (SP4) with its
  Amendments (the critique SP5 and the reviews R1–R3; R4's findings are in the workload README's "What the reviews
  found, and what changed"), `design_model.py` and `estimate_ops.py`; SP3 is `workloads/sparseparity/proto/RESULTS.md`.
- **Tools** ([`workloads/sparseparity/README.md`](../../workloads/sparseparity/README.md) has each one's flags): the
  kernel and host (`kernel/sparseparity.c`, `host/main.cpp`, `host/spp_common.h`, `host/spp_selftest.cpp`); the CPU
  reference and baselines (`cpu/spref.c`, `cpu/spbase.c`, `cpu/sp.h`); `tools/planner.py`, `tools/cycle_model.py`
  (the two-hart cycle model fitted to the card's records), `tools/sptest.py` and `tools/spcore.py`,
  `tools/bench_cpu.py` and `tools/table_cpu.py`, `tools/energy_reduce.py` and `tools/energy_stub.py`; the runners
  `sysemu_check.sh` (every kernel path in `sys_emu`), `card_run.sh` (the card steps M1, the probe, M2, M4 and M5, one
  locked `timeout 10` process each) and `energy.sh` (board energy per solve).
- **Commits:** `92b168f`, `ddb493f`, `4aea801`, `65e6ba6`, `a768f84`, `6082cb9` (the table below).

## A23 — Tracking heat down to transistor flips: the thread's timeline (26 October 2026, Q127)

[Tracking heat down to transistor flips](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/transistor-heat-timeline), built for the
stand-up of 27 October, where the owner had promised to talk about tracking heat generation down to individual
transistor flips. It is A20's shape applied to a whole project rather than to one session: **51 days**, 6 September to
26 October, four tracks on one axis — **the questions** (155 asks of his, each with a twelve-word summary and, in a
reader, its own words), **the sessions** (61 across five machines and two harnesses, grouped into ten named lanes),
**the experiments** (871 intervals in which a card was held, over 48 of the register's experiments, shaded by family)
and **the documents** (39 write-ups, 24 of them public, one lane each, with 374 commits as a tick row). It opens on a
“story so far” of the measured numbers it is about — 116.7 fJ a flipped multiply-add input bit (E60), +0.68 W from
the order of the same arithmetic, ~154 fJ weighed by the camera (E63), 13% then 33% faster on zeros (E64), 1.8 mm of
wire per bit, 1,946 RTL bits toggling for one flip (Q124) — and ends on what is still missing.

**Refreshed for the stand-up, 27 October 02:4x–03:4x, with the night it was itself built in the middle of.**
The thread did not end where the first build stopped, so the page now runs to **52 days** and carries the closing
night: Q126's and Q128's asks in his own words, an **eleventh lane** holding its eight sessions, E65's, E67's and
E68's card spans read from their committed `block.json` and E66 still running on its host — **160 asks, 69
sessions, 952 card intervals over 52 experiments, 94.1 card-hours, 51 write-ups (37 public) and 449 commits**.
The private-archive cross-check is recorded as that and nothing more. The “story so far” and “what's next” are
rebuilt from A24's own public numbers (15% → 33%, 118–136 fJ, 108 electrons, a third of the board accounted for,
×1.147 against a measured 1.127–1.150, 64 bit positions priced and shown not to add), and A24 is linked above
the lede. Six defects of the first build were fixed, the three worth naming here: a redaction that replaced one
name twice and left “a lab user a lab user”; a quoted ask that still named a card host and two repository
folders; and **card-hours stated three ways** — 85 in the lede, 84.6 in the caption and bars that summed to 82 —
so the page now quotes one figure, the hours a card was *held*, to one decimal, and the family chart says plainly
that its bars are the stricter *busy* union. The 19 October talk, where this result was first told to an audience
rather than written down, is now a milestone with no link, no group and no names, because its write-up is private.

**How it differs from A20.** A20's pipeline extracts one session's transcripts on one machine; this thread ran on five
machines under three harnesses, two of which leave no transcript those scripts can read. So the inputs are **curated,
not extracted**: six small JSON files assembled from this repository's own ledgers and from the project's hub, all
committed in `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-transistor-heat-timeline/`, which any checkout can rebuild the page from.
`tools/timeline/build_transistor_timeline.py` turns them into exactly A20's `timeline.json` contract, so the page
script is A20's with the changes listed in its header: the month of a day comes from `meta.day_iso` (A20's heuristic
reads the day number and would mislabel a span crossing two months), the day buttons become week buttons, the four
track headings become QUESTIONS / SESSIONS / EXPERIMENTS / DOCUMENTS, §3 groups by ISO week, the documents lane opens
per page, and the token section is gone, because no token count was collected for this thread. Three blocks are
reinterpreted rather than added: `human` is the questions, `cards` the experiments, `artifacts` the documents.

**The two estimates, said so on the page.** A session's busy time runs from four minutes before each recorded act of
work to twenty-six minutes after it, with gaps under 45 minutes bridged — no harness records attention. An ask's
writing time is its length at 200 characters a minute. Everything else is exact: a card interval is read from the
experiment's own committed data file, and 13 registered experiments that left no timestamps are listed in §5 rather
than drawn.

**The privacy gate.** The builder refuses to write `timeline.json` if the serialised output carries a link on a
private network, a session link, a private address, a home path, an e-mail address, a word about machine access, a
page of the owner's that is not public, a space uuid, a foundry identifier, any mention of the private design archive
beyond the bare fact that a study happened on a date, or a person who is not a published author of cited work (here:
Horace He, Bill Dally and, inside the owner's own words, Mark Horowitz and Jeff Dean). The thirteen private-archive
sessions are one lane with dates and spans and nothing else. Checks on the day: both `check_page.sh` passes read OK at
1280 and 390 px, light and dark; `gestures/suite.mjs` is clean on two phone profiles and the desktop;
`gestures/prompts.mjs` passes every check but the one that tests A20's second message list, which this page has none
of. The E63 page is linked: its space read `signed_in` when the documents were gathered at 16:58 PDT and `public`
when the page was built, because a Q126 pass had re-tiered it at 16:49 (see the last note in this file).

amendment — was redacted the same day in `90693c8a`, though the handles remain in the git history of the
seven commits before it, which only a history rewrite of a public repository would clear.

Harness: `tools/claims-v3/tr/` |

| The glitch factor, measured | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-glitch/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-glitch/README.md) | the SDF-annotated run against its zero-delay twin per scope, the cross-instrument spread, and the split of the step into its measured switching half and its scaled internal half |
| The unpriced capacitance, priced | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-unpriced-capacitance/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-unpriced-capacitance/README.md) | the net-by-net account of the 49.5%: the idle and tied-constant share, the renumbered-bus row, the 252 nets place-and-route created, and the mis-priced join it corrected |
| The ledger's scope: the lane's transport | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-ledger-scope/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-ledger-scope/README.md) | `ledger-scope.json`: the register files, operand bus, result path and control priced, the zero-operand floor split into clock and signal, and the card's own zeros bound that the model violates |
| The rail ledger | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-rail-ledger/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-rail-ledger/README.md) | `rail-ledger.json`: the measured minion rail per multiply-add, the four rows of the board-to-rail step, the two withdrawn rows, and the three-footing leakage sensitivity |
| Stop 9: phonons, fins and the floor | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-stop9-atoms/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-stop9-atoms/README.md) | the liberty pin the 25,000× prices, the two transistor-only rungs beside it, the channel's share of a local flip's heat, and the optical-phonon count per edge |
| Every aifoundry3 reduction at the measured slope | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-leak-sensitivity/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-leak-sensitivity/README.md) | the four per-experiment `leak-sensitivity-e71.{txt,json}` files, `q128-data-at-0.3803.json` and `q128-data-diff.txt` (275 numbers move; the waterfall and the whole bottom-up ledger are byte-identical), plus the reproduction checks at each published slope |
| The governor's temperature term | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-governor-temperature/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-governor-temperature/README.md) | model E64-T1 over both its declared and its unidentified parameters, the out-of-sample misses, and the statement that γ and the thermal trip are jointly unidentified |
| 🛑 The gate every raised-cap arm must pass | [`reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-throttle-wrap-gate/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-throttle-wrap-gate/README.md) | the public half of a firmware hazard corrected in mechanism, field and direction: a 16-bit cast **wraps** at 65.536 W, so on an affected build the power arm **inverts**. Every citation is in the open `et-platform` repository |
| The waterfall, rebuilt | [`reports/data/2026-10-28-q129-waterfall/`](../reports/data/2026-10-28-q129-waterfall/README.md) | `waterfall.py` and `waterfall-v2.json`: the ledger assembled from the lanes above, with the two closures against the board, the glitch split and the reconciliation of the two roads asserted in code (the build fails if they disagree by more than 0.02 pJ) |

Nothing in these eighteen directories names a person, an address, a path into a private tree, or a foundry, process
or library identifier beyond the open ASAP7 kit and the open `et-platform` repository. The archive cross-check that
ran beside them is **not** here, by design; its one publishable half is the throttle-wrap gate above.

## Q130's lanes published no new page: sixteen data directories, seven harness trees, one RTL bench, and two pages corrected in place; the addendum adds six data directories, the real tiles and the kinds page (31 October 2026)

Q130's lanes built **no new space**. Everything they made is in this repository, plus two in-place corrections of
published pages, so — as with Q126 and Q129 — a reader who knows only the published pages will not find it. The
blog-material page, built by Q130's integration lane, is A25 (above); E84's and E85's rows below carry their verified
results, and the addendum after the table records the evening round. The short names are the ones [05-claims.md](05-claims.md), "Q130", uses.

now carries the page's own 38.29 / 46.73 / 63.40 W, from the new `card_description` key in
`docs/reports/sources/horace-experiment.meta.json` (`17efbe77`; the page's bytes unchanged, version `e2a4e30fd570`).

its "~1,050 toggles" label corrected against Q126(b) (`36aaef68`, version `f73a3a4ac4b8`, GitHub Pages `9565edd6`),
then its two corrected facts made to cite the recount and its "65 wires" made 65 merged nets (`cb1f6e4d`, version
`1bc01254c64a`, GitHub Pages `e9afd4d4`; `check-mirror --via https` exit 0); [MIRROR.md](../reports/MIRROR.md) records
both deploys and the test recipe (`ca86be15`, `22c98029`). The memory levels page was not redeployed. Three data
directories' text was corrected as well: the Q129 ledger-scope directory no longer calls the global-route estimate a
lower bound on the wire (`88e2f336`), and the toggle recount's and the routed netlist's READMEs now link the fixes that
answer them (`99295452`).

Q130(g), sourced only to public material.

### The addendum: the evening round's six data directories and the real tiles (31 October 2026, 18:39–22:16 PDT)

experiments, each with its harness committed before its claim, then verified and corrected in its own commit. Their
claims are in [05-claims.md](05-claims.md), "Q130 additions"; A25 (above) carries them since its rebuild at 22:53 PDT.

## E135 published no new page: one data directory and the GPU harness (2 November 2026)

E135, at the owner's OK of 2 November, ran on rented A100s on Modal, so it holds no lab card and no card post. What it made is in this repository; the kinds page (A25) took it first, rebuilt in place as `9fdb534bbc77` (A25's dated note), and the Horace hub in its own pass. The short name is the one [05-claims.md](05-claims.md), "E135", uses. "Item 2" is the kinds page's section 11, "A GPU measured the same way", and the data README's first lines name it.

| What | Where | What is in it |
|---|---|---|
| **E135**, Horace He's kinds of numbers on two A100-SXM4-40GB boards at their 400 W limit, burst and sustained, NVML every 100 ms (item 2) | [`reports/data/2026-11-02-e135-a100-kinds/`](../reports/data/2026-11-02-e135-a100-kinds/README.md) (`ffbcd3d8`, `0e7cc89d`, `57809cdc`) | `results.json` and `reduce.txt` (the registered reduction: cells, gaps, replicates, his loop, the lower cap, R1–R10, checks), `posthoc.json` and `posthoc.txt` (no verdicts), `smoke-reduce.txt`, `billing.json` (the metered cost by app and resource, and the GPU-seconds), `rebuild.sh` (every derived file from `logs/`, Python 3 only), `corrections-check.py` and `.txt`, and `logs/` (the measured run's steps and 100 ms samples, compressed, byte-identical to the Modal Volume, and the declared smoke) |
| The GPU harness | [`tools/gpu/e135/`](../../tools/gpu/e135/E135.md) (`89378ba6`, A1 `73fc73a7`) | `E135.md` (the registration, frozen), `predictions-e135.json` and `.txt` with `make_predictions_e135.py`, `e135_app.py` (the Modal app: pinned image, the two units' plans, the budget guard and timeouts), `reduce_e135.py`, `selftest_e135.py` and `selftest.txt`, `posthoc_e135.py` (written after the registration; its sections 6–7 after the data) |

E135 sent nothing to any ET-SoC-1. On the GPU it read NVML getters only; its one set, the lower cap, was refused (exit 4), and nothing needed restoring. The board's identity is a 12-hex hash of its UUID; the `nvidia-smi -q` text kept in `logs/` also holds the GPU PDI, the Board ID and the PCI bus id, none a secret (C11). The Modal Volume `e135-a100-kinds` still holds the raw logs.

Dated notes added by these records: E153's README (its C-checked launches are the first checked reads on aifoundry3, E160 having
read pool 6's new ranges first, E157's C2) and Q139(e)'s README (E97's 0.3–0.8 °C is a move within its one session, E144's C3, as
E154's C13 found).
