## The camera and the details panel (30 September)

For Q74–Q76: the memory levels and the chip diagram (the pages of 28 and 27 September) jumped in the middle of their
camera moves. The diagnosis, the method and the numbers are in
[`../reports/data/2026-09-30-page-motion/`](../reports/data/2026-09-30-page-motion/README.md); the tools in
[`tools/pagemotion/`](../../tools/pagemotion/README.md).

- **Memory levels** (`sources/memory-levels.*`): the camera moves in legs, each one pure zoom along a chain of scales
  in its root's coordinates (no turn and no change of pace at a scale passed; the scales' real sizes replace the
  nominal `tw`); two legs meet at rest; each view's fade, labels and context follow from where the camera is, an
  outer view staying until the inner covers the screen; no text halos while moving (`#mem.zmv`), faded labels not
  painted (`.nolab`), `text-rendering: geometricPrecision`, a 40 ms per-frame clock cap. The playback bar moved to the
  top with a scrubber (one segment per step or tour slide; click or drag) and the keys beside it. All 28 accesses were
  replayed with Dive on without a console error.
- **Chip diagram** (`sources/chip-diagram.*`): its drive runs legs, easing once through every scale's rest view and
  blending the turn there (`BLEND` 0.45); out and in meet at rest; the 40 ms cap. The details panel leads with a
  plain-words line for each part (`LEADS`) and flow (`FLOWLEAD`); the technical paragraph, the numbers, the address
  bits, the tables, the asks and the facts sit under "More detail and the sources", closed until the reader opens it
  (remembered in `localStorage`); flow 5's title lost its experiment ID.
- Both deployed on 30 September (the versions "Smooth camera: …"); the live pages equal their files.

## The deep zoom (30 September, branch `chipzoom`; merged 750a6ed and deployed 1 October)

For Q77, the owner's requests about the chip diagram: double-click everything that can be selected, a wide button up
the hierarchy, zoom out to the rack, Studio 45, San Francisco, the US, Earth, the Milky Way, "the Andromeda Cluster"
and "the meta universe", zoom in to circuit components, and the shires' edge arrows as links. The design (DESIGN.md
in the session's work directory, `~/claude/work/chipzoom/`) was written and reviewed first; it is followed here.

- **The shires' arrows** (`5985d6d`): each edge label is a link (role link, Tab, Enter, a 44 px hit area on touch).
  Since the path camera, a compute shire, the master or spare, a memory shire, the PCIe or the I/O shire is reached by
  a sideways glide (two layers side by side, about 0.65 s); the arrow keys do the same when no flow or tour is on.
- **The path camera** (`6e17b89`, a pure refactor checked against the camera of the morning: identical frame counts
  and late frames on flows 1, 8 and B, the velocity kinks within 0.5 points): the camera rests on a path of scales and
  computes every frame in the coordinates of the step it is in, so 36 powers of ten fit; nest, jump and pan steps.
- **The controls** (`8567a9e`): the Up bar (a wide "↑ Zoom out to …" button, the breadcrumb with a "…" menu, a scale
  readout), click selects and double-click (double-tap, Enter, +) zooms, a "Zoom in" pill on touch, the panel's zoom
  row and its "You are here" entry, `?at=`.
- **The levels** (`sources/chip-diagram.outside.js`, `sources/circuitkit.js`, `sources/chip-diagram.inside.js`; the
  data in `data/2026-09-27-chip-diagram/research/`: `inside.json`, `outside.json`, `outside-geo.json`, their builders
  and write-ups, `deepzoom.py`, `make_circuitkit.py`, `make_rack_photo.py`): 18 levels above the die (maps from the US
  Census and Natural Earth, NASA's images, the vendor's card photo, the owner's rack photo with ten machine labels
  blurred, Studio 45 named but not placed), and below it the memory levels' drawings (copied by `make_circuitkit.py`,
  the memory-levels page untouched), logical drawings of the blocks built from the tree's data, and a hand-drawn compute
  path from a lane's fused multiply-add to the silicon crystal. Every number a drawing prints is checked against its
  fact by the build (`ONUM`, `INUM`, the memory levels' numbers imported as `ml:`). Kinds: one vocabulary of nine.
- **Deploy form**: a folder (`index.html` and `chip-diagram-img/`, six WebP images loaded only near their levels);
  MIRROR.md's row says so. Not deployed from the branch.
- **Tests** (`tools/pagemotion/zoom_test.mjs`, T1-T7, T9, T11; `zoom_static.py`, T9 and T10) and motion
  (`drive.mjs`): in the commit messages.
- **Final check (1 October)**: rebuilt from sources byte for byte; a scripted tour with real input on a desktop and a
  phone (three part types and the mesh double-clicked, two dives to a transistor, Up pressed 29 times to the top and
  back down, the four edge arrows of shire 20) passed 131 of 131 checks each. It fixed block boxes that ended on a
  colon ("lanes:"), two image credits that ran past the drawing's edge, and a legible port label in the rack photo.

## The shared ladder, part 1 (1 October, branch `ladder`; deployed 1 October, 65a06da, and 2 October, 302fe0e)

The owner's request of 1 October (Q88): make the chip diagram and the memory levels consistent, give the memory levels
the Up button and the same scales, go down to transistors, atoms and quarks, close the ladder into a circle past the
observable universe, place Studio 45 on 29th Street in Bernal Heights, research TSMC N7 and the ET-SoC-1, and fill in the
electronics; and at 07:25 (Q89): everything above the rack is an easter egg, never named in advance. Part 1 builds the
shared modules and the chip diagram's use of them; the memory levels move onto them next. The design is in the work
directory (`~/claude/work/ladder/DESIGN.md`); the data's README (`docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/README.md`) keeps
what the repository needs of it.

- **Shared include files** (`docs/reports/sources/`): `ladder-core.js` (the path camera, the Up bar, breadcrumb and
  readout, the wrap, the easter egg, the two-state switch, the lazy data), `ladder-panel.js` (zoom rows, gestures,
  tooltips), `ladder-outer.js` (the levels outside the chip), `ladder-mem.js` (the memory chains as scales),
  `ladder-inner.js` (the circuits, the device at N7, the atom to the Planck length, the ring), `ladder.css`; the chip's
  own `chip-diagram.blocks.js`. `scripts/build-report.py` expands nested and CSS includes and refuses a name declared
  twice at the top level and a page without the core's hooks.
- **The data**: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/` (`build_ladder.py`, `ladder.json`; the research on N7, Esperanto,
  the particles and Studio 45 with their builders). The chip's deep-zoom facts are fetched after the first paint from
  `docs/reports/ladder-img/ladder-data.json` (the images folder, renamed from `chip-diagram-img/`), which keeps the page
  at 1.31 MB.
- **What a reader sees**: Up past the rack only says "?" and goes on to Studio 45 (a 37 by 9 m building, schematic), a
  side view of 29th Street, Bernal Heights (DataSF's outlines; the whole street, no point on it) and the city, and on to
  the universe; past the top, a ring of sizes ("a picture, not a place"), then in again at the Planck length and up
  through a quark, a proton, a nucleus, an atom, the crystal, the channel, the fin and the FinFET into a compute gate or
  a 6T cell. Down from the die: the FinFET at N7's pitches, the fin 6 by 52 nm under its gate stack, the channel 16.5 nm
  (257,000 atoms), the crystal and its band gap, an atom, its nucleus, a proton, a quark, the Planck length, with an
  electron, a phosphorus dopant and a DRAM cell beside them. The gate switch (G) draws the device on and off; the
  inverter's input and a stored bit switch too.
- **The owner's second update (Q90, 09:00)**: every sideways move has its way back (the memory, PCIe and I/O shires got
  the shire's edge links: all 44 cells of the die link to each neighbour, and each link has its link back; the arrow keys
  too); the loop (Up from the ring lands on one fixed atom, a silicon atom in the channel of a FinFET of an XOR in a full
  adder of the compressor tree of lane 0's multiply-add, and Up from it climbs to the chip, the rack and round again;
  the ring's panel also offers an atom of a 6T cell and the Planck length); completeness (`ladder-circuits.js`: 17
  textbook constructions, each labelled "textbook construction · this chip's own circuit: not published", down to a gate
  drawn as transistors; every part of the chip's drawings leads further in, the few that are not the ET-SoC-1's, the
  host's processor, memory and supply and the card's regulators and switches, say so). The data:
  `docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/research/build_circuits.py`. The tests: `zoom_test.mjs` T4b (every cell's links
  and their ways back, by click, Enter and the arrow keys), T17 (the loop: Up from the die passes the ring twice and
  lands on the same atom), T18 (the navigation graph: every scale reached, every zoom with a seat, no one-way exit) and
  T19 (no dead ends: every part zooms in, every scale reaches a transistor and then an atom; a vector add by
  double-clicks from the chip to an atom).
- **Part 1b, completeness (Q90, afternoon)**: a walk of every scene by its path (`__chipTest.walk()`: 316 paths of 86
  scales, 2,863 parts from the rack down; part 1's check had looked at one path per scale) found six parts with no zoom
  at all (the host's processor, memory and supply, the card's core and SRAM regulators and its boot switches), four
  zooms from a block straight to the FinFET with no gate drawn in transistors between (the wiring stack's wires and
  bumps, the PLL's charge pump and loop filter) and blocks standing in for whole circuits (a PCIe lane opened an
  inverter, the crossbars a 2:1 multiplexer, the multiply-add's rounding the adder, the DQ pin's receiver a FinFET).
  Nine scenes close them (`ladder-circuits.js`): a PCIe lane as a SerDes, block by block (eight lanes, each with its
  way sideways), a differential pair and the CTLE made of it, a crossbar of multiplexers and arbiters, rounding to
  nearest even, copper wiring in section and a copper atom, a buck converter, a trench power transistor (the
  regulators' own, never the N7 FinFET), and a boot-strap pin with the chip's receiver and flip-flop; the PLL's pump and
  filter are drawn as transistors; the host's parts open the constructions they share with the chip's, each panel saying
  it is not the ET-SoC-1's. Only the die's key, a legend, has no zoom. The data: `research/build_circuits.py` (45 more
  facts, among them PCIe 4.0's equalisers and loss budget, the card's TPSM831D31 and LTM4680, the chip's 11 boot-pin
  bits, copper's atom and free electrons, Intel 14 nm's pitches). The tests: `zoom_test.mjs` T20 (every scene by its
  path: every part leads in with its seat in that drawing, every zoom to a transistor comes from a drawing in
  transistors, every scale reaches a transistor and an atom, the DRAM's and the regulators' chains never reach the N7
  FinFET) and T19's double-click chains for a PCIe lane, the boot switches and the core regulator.
- **The owner's third update (1 Oct, about 17:10; `b88da9b` on main, the memory levels in `11b6e45`)**: "When zooming
  up, skip this slide [the ring of sizes] ... it should directly go at the lowest level ... The loop should always go in
  one direction." Since then Up from the top (beyond) goes straight to the Planck length under the loop's fixed atom, in
  one cross-fade (the readout "↻ round the loop: 10²⁶ m → 10⁻³⁵ m"), and on up through a quark, a proton, the nucleus
  and that atom to the chip and round again: 40 presses a lap from the die, the same atom every lap; + from a Planck
  length goes to the top. The ring of sizes is off the loop: only its link in the top's and the Planck length's panels,
  or `#at=p.wrap`, opens it. The landing panel says the loop is a picture, not a journey through space (as in Glashow's
  uroboros), and names the fixed point (on the chip, a silicon atom in the channel of a FinFET of an XOR gate, in a full
  adder of a 4:2 compressor of lane 0's multiply-add). The bullets above that say "Up from the ring" describe the loop
  before this update. Tests: `zoom_test.mjs` T3, T5, T7, T12, T17, T18; `ml_ladder_test.mjs` T5, T12, T17.

## The shared ladder, part 2: the memory levels (1 October, branch `ladder`; deployed 2 October, 302fe0e)

The owner's request of 1 October (Q88), its easter egg (Q89) and its second update (Q90) on "Anatomy of a memory
access, interactively" (`docs/reports/sources/memory-levels.*`): the chip diagram's Up bar, breadcrumb and readout, the
same scales outward and inward, the loop, a way back from every move and no dead end, consistent with the chip diagram.

- **Two cameras, one ladder** (`memory-levels.ladder.js`, `.nodes.js`, `.links.js`, `.hand.js`): the levels keep their own
  camera for their scenes (tabs, the 28 accesses and Dive, the phone's sideways window, the cross-fades between the maps);
  the chip diagram's path camera runs beside it in a scope of its own, on its own SVG over the same box, for every scale
  outside them, and the two hand over at rest on a scene both draw with the levels' own builder (the pictures coincide
  to the pixel; on a phone the same sideways window). The design's one camera (D2) was set aside: the levels' camera cannot
  span 45 decades, and a port of the accesses, Dive and the phone window risked the smoothness the owner had asked for;
  its fallback (the outer and inner scales only on the chip page) would not have met the request.
- **What a reader sees**: ↑ from the L1's minion goes out to the L2's shire, the chip level's map, the package, the
  card, the host, the rack and then "?" (the easter egg, as on the chip; since the third update, above, Up from the
  top goes straight round to the Planck length under an atom of the reader's level's own cell, the L1's latch, a 6T
  cell of the L2, the L3 or the scratchpad, the DRAM's cell through the DRAM process's transistor, never N7's, and
  climbs back through that cell to the level and round again: 35 presses a lap from the L3's cell). + and
  double-clicks go down every part: the levels' own scales where they draw one, else the chip diagram's scene for it
  (the textbook constructions, the block scenes), down to a transistor, its fin and channel, the crystal, an atom, its
  nucleus, a proton, a quark and the Planck length. A click selects and a double-click (a double-tap) zooms, as on the
  chip; a part's panel has the chip's shape (its lead, where its double-click goes, the rest folded); after a move,
  "You are here". One word for each kind of fact; the vector unit and the TensorLoad unit coloured as logic, the map's
  cells without a compute shire labelled. On a phone the accesses keep one row, the address one line (fields ▾) and
  Fit to screen sits in the Up bar: the drawing starts 32 px higher than before.
- **Tests** (`tools/pagemotion/`): `ml_ladder_test.mjs` (Up level by level to the package and the rack, + from the top to
  the Planck length and round, reduced motion, the ring's ways in per level, the easter egg, the two states, the loop, the
  navigation graph, no dead ends over a walk of every scene, the hand-over by crumb, tab and Play, a part of the levels'
  drawings double-clicked into the ladder's scale, the names against the chip diagram at ten shared places, the
  hand-over under quick input, the panel on the ladder's copies of the levels' scenes; desktop and phone),
  `ml_access_test.mjs` (all 28 accesses with Dive play to their end, go out to the package and come back to play
  again), `ui_test.mjs` (the player bar, as before); `zoom_static.py` checks the memory levels too (their budgets:
  2.0 MB, script 1,050 KB, data 800 KB). Results on the final build: `ml_ladder_test.mjs` 108 of 108 on the desktop and
  on a phone, `ml_access_test.mjs --dive` 28 of 28, `ui_test.mjs` as before; the chip diagram's `zoom_test.mjs` 667 of
  667 and 504 of 504, its pictures unchanged to the pixel; `zoom_static.py` 0 failed on both (the memory levels 1.90 MB,
  script 1,016 KB); `check_page.sh` OK, light and dark; the other 15 pages rebuild byte for byte.
- **The levels' own suites of 30 September** (the level moves: 43 runs of the porter's and the reviewers' suites, the
  keys, the accesses, the tour, races, the phone's taps and swipes, fuzzers and a leak test) on the new page against the
  committed one (`docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/motion/regress-p2.txt`): the same but where the page changed by
  design (no mini-map: the Up bar replaces it; a tap selects and a double-tap zooms, as on the chip; no scale control).
  Found and fixed on the way: `__memState().zooming` did not count the ladder's moves, so a test waiting for rest could
  look in the middle of a hand-over; a tab clicked while a move of the ladder waited for the levels' camera lost its
  level to it; under reduced motion a link left the stage with the ladder; and the ladder's copy of a levels' scene that
  is not their place (another bank, another shire) kept the panel of the place left (T7, T23, T24).
- **Motion** (`docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/motion/README.md`, part 2): the levels' own camera moves as before
  (the access `#dram/load` and the level moves: 0 late frames of 625 and 447 at 1x on both pages, the same velocity
  kinks; at 4x both pages drop frames alike); the chip diagram's flows are the base's frame for frame; the new flows (Up
  from the L3's map to the rack and back, + from the L2's cell to an atom and back) have no late frame of 357 and 470 at
  1x since the take-over got two frames of its own (before, the hand-over's move began with a jump, 58% of its top speed
  in one frame). First load on a phone profile at 4x CPU: first paint +7%, DOMContentLoaded +13% (the design's ±10% is
  missed by 3 points: 0.73 MB more to parse, of which the ladder's start-up takes 30-45 ms).
- **Not done**: the levels' maps are not turned to the die's orientation, and the minions' order in a neighbourhood is

  scene that is not their example (another bank, another shire) has no sideways window of its own; links between the
  two pages that carry a place wait on the viewer (below).
- **Links between the two pages that carry a place** (DESIGN §3.8) were not made: the spacesheep viewer passes neither
  `?at=` nor `#at=` into the page it frames (checked on the public pages, 1 October).
- **The four reviews of 1 October evening and the final check** (`11b6e45`, and the final check's commit of 2 October;
  the data README's "Part 3" and "The final check"): the memory levels on main's one-way loop (the third update, above),
  the facts corrected, the levels' controls made the chip's, the circuit drawings put right; then, in the final check,
  `tools/publish-gh-pages.sh` copies `ladder-img/` beside the memory levels too (their lazy facts and images would have
  answered 404 on GitHub Pages), and the chip diagram's address writer no longer draws every scene on the way to check an

  `zoom_test.mjs` 783/783 and 620/620, `ml_ladder_test.mjs --chip` 150/150 and 137/137, `ml_access_test.mjs` 28/28 with
  and without Dive, both pages rebuilt byte for byte, `check_page.sh` OK light and dark; motion against `bb0eb78` in
  `docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/motion/README.md`, "The final check".
- **− and + keep their place (13 October, Q107).** The owner: "Make sure that the plus and minuses don't move." In both
  pages' Up bar, − and + sat between the crumbs and the readout and slid with the readout's width; they now come last,
  at the right end of the crumbs' row, the readout gives way last (an ellipsis, rather than push them off the bar), and
  under 900 px wide the rows keep their heights and never wrap (`ladder.css`, both `body.html`; the ladder's README,
  "− and + keep their place"). Both pages rebuilt byte for byte from their sources before the change, so the built
  diff is the change alone; both spaces redeployed from the committed pages, and the GitHub Pages mirror rebuilt. The
  measurement, before and after: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-13-zoom-buttons/`.
- **The readout's one look, smoother moves, Studio 45 from its plans (13 October, Q108).** The owner: "The text
  sometimes changes in size" and "the animation is sometimes not smooth". The readout no longer grows and turns bold
  while the camera moves (`ladder.css`); the playback bar's hint keeps the stages' room, so the drawing keeps its height
  when a flow starts (`chip-diagram.body.html`); on a wide screen the drawing's box is held at its size in pixels, so a
  change in the Up bar no longer lays the drawing out again, and the next scale is drawn in an idle moment
  (`ladder-core.js`, `pinView` and `prebuildNext`); Studio 45 is drawn from the venue's measured plans, both floors and
  a section, the lab upstairs (`ladder-outer.js`; facts s45.9–s45.17). Both pages rebuilt from their sources with the
  ladder's facts chain at its fixed point; both spaces redeployed from the committed pages, and the GitHub Pages mirror

  "The readout's one look, smoother moves, Studio 45 from its plans".
- **A correctness check, and Codex's review folded in (14 October, Q115).** The owner: "Do the in-depth check of the
  chip for correctness ... and apply fixes", then "Go through the [Codex] suggestions/fixes and implement everything which
  is appropriate." Every fact, panel, flow stage, tour slide and drawing checked against the manuals, the core-et documents
  and RTL, the firmware and this repository: 97 findings stand (11 major), 2 refuted; 181 drifted line citations settled;
  Codex's 68 observations: 40 implemented, 7 covered, 4 rejected, 17 left for the owner. The fixes went into the sources
  (the research builders and JSON, `build_facts.py`'s tables, the page scripts, the memory levels' kit, the ladder's
  research), both pages and the 3D companion were rebuilt, the chain at its fixed point (`buildchain.sh` reproduced the
  committed pages byte for byte before the first edit), the page crawled again and diffed, five auditors checked the result,
  and the suites pass on the final build. The record, the tools, the crawl diff and every finding with its verdict:
  `docs/reports/data/2026-10-14-chip-check/`. That evening, the same Discussion tab's two Antigravity audits (topics 3
  and 4: layout, type and interaction): their 41 claims measured in the built page at 1440 × 900, at 1280 × 720 and on
  a phone, 13 real (fixed by nine changes: frame titles kept clear of their tags, the lines, notes and labels that ran
  past the frame on short screens and phones fitted, a label's knockout in the tint it sits on, the stored bit's notes
  kept in their box, a phone's missing "sea level", the 3D link following the panel), 3 already fixed, 15 wrong when
  measured, 10 left for the owner; five more such defects found while measuring were fixed too. Measured over all 104
  scales before and after: 27 texts past a frame at 1280 × 720 and 29 on a phone fixed, none new
  (`antigravity-triage.json`, `antigravity-measure.md` there).
- **TensorFMA32: the multiply-add as built, and a twelfth flow (25 October, Q124).** The owner asked for the TensorFMA32
  part of a private copy of this page (made there on 23 October from Esperanto's design files for the ET-SoC-1). Flow M
  (key M) flips one input bit of lane 0's multiply-add and follows it through the seven stages; the deep scenes go down
  the Booth multiplier and the Wallace tree as built (four levels of 64 4:2 compressors) to a full adder's 28
  transistors, and the loop's fixed atom now sits in that full adder. The copy's page was carried back into the sources
  hunk by hunk (each line of a built page traced to the source line it came from), its data into
  `research/tensorfma32.json`; the shared ladder's files changed with it, so the memory levels show the same scenes and
  print the chip's numbers they need. Its sources say "Esperanto's design files for the ET-SoC-1" beside core-et's open
  Erbium RTL, which has every module and signal named; no design file is named and no cell-library dimension appears.
  Thirteen of the copy's new parts led nowhere; each now leads in (a stage's header where its first block does, the
  Booth groups and the full adder's stages where they are clicked, a cut compressor into its column), and the Booth
  multiplier's and the tree's glides to each other are reported as sideways glides, which T18 checks for their way back.
  The suites on the final build: `zoom_test.mjs` 791/791, `ml_ladder_test.mjs --chip` 149/150 (its one miss, T13's breadcrumb at shire 0, fails the same on the unchanged pages on this Mac), `ml_access_test.mjs` 28/28.
- **The extremes as pictures (31 October – 1 November, Q135).** The owner: "Improve the images at the higher end of the
  scale, milky way, observable universe, etc, by generating appropriate prompts, generating images using AGy/antigravity
  and using them in place of current diagrams ... Look at the extreme levels only (ie, above studio 45, and below
  individual atoms)". Thirteen pictures drawn by Antigravity from written prompts carry the levels in space (Beyond to
  Earth) and below the atom (the nucleus to the Planck length) on both pages that share the ladder; each scene keeps its
  seats, rings, scale bars and numbers over its picture, tagged "AI illustration" and credited in its panel. The maps
  stay drawn. Where each scene places its picture, and why: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-01-ladder/images/README.md`.
  Tests on the final build, run on the mini from python's http.server, one run at a time: `zoom_test.mjs` 789 of 793 on the desktop and 626 of 630 on a phone, `ml_ladder_test.mjs` 107 of 139 on the desktop and 100 of 126 on a phone. Every miss is a "no console errors" check catching that server's 404 for `/favicon.ico`, which it does not serve: the committed build before the change fails T7 the same way, and the server's request log names no other 404; so nothing the change touches failed. `zoom_static.py` 0 failed on both pages; `check-mirror.py` exit 0 on both. Live as the chip diagram's `a85232dc109f` and the memory levels' `4a034260b2f4`; the GitHub Pages mirror rebuilt.

## Four physical scales in 3D (14 October 2026, Q109)

[Inside the silicon](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-in-3d) (public, unlisted; space `9018f695-7165-4852-9fbb-4f49cfcaefa1`) adds four orbitable 3D teaching models: metal tracks and vias, a FinFET with its gate and oxide separated, parallel silicon fins, and an n-channel device under qualitative gate drive with positive drain bias. The chip diagram links each model from its corresponding scale panel and from a four-view index.

The initial standalone canvas source was `docs/reports/chip-3d.html`. Q113 replaces it with generated, self-contained WebGL HTML, built from `docs/reports/sources/chip-3d/` using `npm ci` and `node scripts/build-chip-3d.mjs`; Three.js is bundled, with no runtime downloads. All lengths, charge and motion are illustrative; the models do not reconstruct ET-SoC-1 physical design. The QA/reproduction note is `docs/reports/data/2026-10-14-chip-3d/README.md`. GitHub Pages publishes the companion under `chip-3d/` through `tools/publish-gh-pages.sh`.

**Perspective corrected (14 October, Q110).** The companion's first camera refitted every rotated screen-space outline, which changed magnification without changing the zoom state. Its camera now orbits a fixed world-space target at fixed distance and focal scale. Scene bounds are measured once over the full control range. Pointer drag, keyboard orbit, presets, auto-orbit and geometry controls retain magnification; explicit zoom and viewport resizing behave independently. `camera-test.mjs` reproduced the old 13.29% scaling error and passed the correction on all four scenes at desktop and phone widths.

**Gate labels stabilized (14 October, Q112).** The FinFET and channel views now keep their gate, source and drain callouts in fixed viewport slots, with leaders to physical projected anchors. The old collision solver moved the gate in 25-pixel increments; `label-test.mjs` reproduced a 50-pixel jump. Full rotation, pitch and zoom sweeps on desktop/phone show zero text-position and camera-magnification drift. Mobile gate labels are visible; the channel callout attaches to the electrode top.

**Research-based visual upgrade (14 October, Q113).** Intel's tri-gate figures, Hu's fin/charge sections and imec's interconnect illustrations informed original detailed geometry: lined copper vias, local-to-upper wiring hierarchy, device contacts, raised source/drain, high-k shell, spacers, STI, shared-gate fins and finite inversion volumes. WebGL adds rounded geometry, studio reflections, soft cached shadows, correct depth testing, raycast picking and fixed callouts on all four views. Interactive cutaways and synchronized labeled SVG sections explain material interfaces and the approximate gated-perimeter relation. Camera and label fixes remain enforced. Eight 1280/390px interaction cases passed; a full-turn keyboard sweep and sampled auto-orbit frames had zero magnification/label drift. Two interleaved before/after frame runs had 16.7ms median; new-renderer p95 was 16.8ms with one 66–83ms outlier in each run. No calibrated voltage/current or proprietary geometry is asserted. Research and tests live beside the README.

**Codex's review applied (14 October, Q115).** Five of the observations in Codex's audit of the chip diagram concern the
companion and were applied in its sources (`docs/reports/sources/chip-3d/`), the page rebuilt with `node
scripts/build-chip-3d.mjs` (its committed sources first rebuilt byte for byte): the metal scene's cutaway now opens with
the dielectric shown, and hiding the dielectric closes it (the button had announced a change the model did not make);
Ctrl, Cmd and Alt with a key stay the browser's; "Reset" reads "Reset view", which is what it does; the live line says
"Cutaway closed." instead of "Assembled view."; and the pulses and carriers keep their place when a slider moves or the
flow is paused. The decisions and their evidence: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-14-chip-check/codex-triage.json` (I02, I03,
I10, I11, I12).

## The 24 September review

On the evening of 24 September 2026 every page the hub linked was reviewed for consistency, cross-links, suspicious
claims and readability by a workflow of AI agents. Each finding was verified against the raw data, the firmware or
the RTL before it was applied, and the corrected numbers were recomputed from the files in this repository. What
the review changed:

**Every page was revised.** Each keeps its space and slug. "Text in" names where the corrected text lives
(`sources/` is `docs/reports/sources/`); the built or edited HTML is the file in the tables above.

## The 25 September validation

On 25 September 2026 the repo owner asked for a full validation of the 18 public pages: re-run the scripts, recheck
the claims, remove redundancy, reorganise where needed and add interactive visualisations. A workflow of AI agents
ran, for 11 page groups, a local reproduction, a page review and an independent verification of each finding, plus
one review of the set's structure, and wrote the plan (`PLAN2.md`); page owners then applied it. The record is in
[`docs/reports/data/2026-09-24-report-review/`](../reports/data/2026-09-24-report-review/README.md), beside the
24 September plan. Nothing in it used a card: simulator checks ran in a sandbox with a private `/dev`.

- **The checker was wrong, and was fixed first.** `check_page.sh` injected its error listener just before
  `</body>`, after a page's own scripts had run, so an exception thrown while a page loads was never seen: every
  "no JS errors" of 24 September was unproven. It now installs the listener right after `<head>`. Rerun on the 18
  committed pages at `08076ae`, at 1280 and 390 px, in light, `data-theme` dark and the OS dark preference: 108 of
  108 checks OK, no load-time errors. Positive control: the DVFS page built from a `dvfs.json` without its `cards`
  block (175,680 characters) throws a `TypeError` that the fixed checker reports and the old one did not (it saw
  only three empty fields). The copy in the review record's `tools/` is the fixed one, with `shot.sh` beside it.
- **What reproduced.** Every analysis that runs without a card was rerun, and nearly everything reproduced byte
  for byte: the catalogue, the reruns, the energy manual's data, markdown and page; `wire.json`, `report.json` and
  the heat-per-mm page; the whole Horace chain (`finish_horace.sh`); `dvfs.json`; `onchip.json`; the memory-anatomy
  summary and page; the four 2026-09-18 embeds and the ridge-points page; the sandboxed `sys_emu` checks (for the
  test drive, 524,359 "type A" VPU register-file warnings at n = 64 on one shire: 524,291 on the compute shire, two
  for each of the 262,144 `fmadd.s`, plus 3 from `fsw`; the master shire and the service processor add 68).
- **What did not, and what was done.** Numbers with no producer script got one: the DDR-droop block (an unrecorded
  inline run) is now regenerated by `fit_unmetered.py` (0.84 → 0.87 mV/W, the owner's decision);
  `transfer_cards.py` writes `transfer.json` and `leakage_crosscard.json`; `build_vf.py` writes `vf.json` (87.8 W
  peak, not 87.6); `analyze_hotline.py --context --barrier` writes the hot line's `context` block (the chip barrier
  4,995 cycles from its raw file, not the hand-entered 4,997); `summarize_power_session.py` and
  `parse_sptrace_voltage.py` rebuild A3's data; `sync_hub_data.py` writes the hub's computed blocks (its fit, droop and
  idle figures had been copied into its data file by hand; A16). Stale reductions were regenerated: `enercat.json`'s 672 rail values
  and the hot line's `power.json` (18 rail values), both made before the `[avg,min,max]` fix of `4845a60`.
  `analyze_dvfs.py` had corrected the wake-up latencies' late carry a second time (the DRAM count is 13 of 20, not
  12) and its documented recipe dropped the `cards` merge; `analyze_catalogue.py` gained the sampler's latency and
  the rails' filter; `analyze_power.py` could not read the committed memory-anatomy data (a CSV fallback now);
  `build_wire_report.py` dropped a section when run outside the repo root; `ridge-points.py` checked its bandwidths
  against runs the energy manual does not pool (0.2% → 0.3%); the review's `fits.txt` was the `--keep-bad` output.
  Not regenerable, and said so: the test drive's silicon SGEMM figures (the host printout was not found).
- **Claims corrected across pages** (PLAN2 §1.2; every finding was verified against the data before it was applied): DRAM rows follow an open-page policy and close
  at a refresh or another row's access, not on a timer; the relay's bandwidth follows the longest hand-off (up to a
  quarter over the offsets tried), not "no measurable difference"; the catalogue keeps the DRAM bursts that slow the
  sampler; stores through the L1 explain the unmetered fit's DRAM residual (the fit is kept as published); the hot
  line's bank rule is necessary, not sufficient; heat per mm's per-second bound holds on the mesh rail only; the
  ridge points' energy conclusion holds for random operands; On-chip communication quotes the 23 September energies.
  Set-wide: board and rail readings change about every 250 ms on aifoundry3 (133 ms is aifoundry2's, PLAN2 D2′); the rails' filter is measured, 55–57% of a step after 1 s and 83–84% after 2 s (τ ≈ 1.15–1.22 s; superseded by E58
  on 29 September: 1.01–1.10 s, one SP pass late, and 0.54 s on aifoundry1's card 1's SRAM rail),
  with the `/0.94` correction kept and its systematic stated (the minion delivery loss moves about 1.2 points per 1%
  of rail scale).
- **Redundancy and structure.** Topics that were told at length on three to eight pages each now have one canonical
  home and short pointers elsewhere (PLAN2 §2.3); several pages were reordered with every heading id kept. **The L2
  mainline-starvation brief became a pointer page** (the owner's decision, IA-07): its unique advice moved to A13,
  its TensorSend layouts to On-chip communication, and the brief as corrected on 24 September stays in git at
  `49dd0c8`.
- **The chart toolkit** (A12) and the visualisations built with it to one standard (PLAN2 §2.5–§2.7): charts sized
  to their container that keep their key feature at phone width, marks reachable from the keyboard, dark mode from
  the theme tokens, and every number read from the page's data.
- **Raw records recovered.** The SP trace dumps behind E6's voltage map (`raw/sp1.bin`, `sp2.bin`, `sp3.bin`) and
  E5's load log (`raw/thermal-loads.log`) were still in aifoundry2's build tree and are committed with checksums in
  `docs/reports/data/2026-09-20-power-aifoundry2/raw/`; `sp1.bin` reproduces the committed map byte for byte.
- **Owner decisions** (PLAN2 §4.2): demote the L2 brief; regenerate the droop block; keep the unmetered fit and
  explain the L1-store residual; adopt the measured τ and keep `/0.94`; qualify 133 ms as aifoundry2's on the hub and
  in the findings; commit the recovered raw records; keep the firmware-version caveat (which et-platform commit the
  cards run is still not established).
- **The last pass, and the deploy.** A final polish (the chart toolkit keeps end-of-axis labels inside the frame;
  Horace's 14 and why-low-power's 2 older charts moved to the toolkit) and its checks turned up four more fixes. The
  s ↔ s+16 ring does not hold the board reading "for seconds": its longest unchanged value is 0.84 s, and what
  changes is the rate, 1.5–2.4 new values a second against 5.3–6.6 for the other rings on aifoundry2 (the hub, the
  manual and `analyze_reruns.py` now say so). The sampler's starved latency is 76–146 ms (per-pass medians), not
  "about 150". The hub's L1 row and the memory-hierarchy page's A100 comparison now use the manual's recommended L1
  figure (unrolled vector loads, 0.54 pJ/B at 14.2 TB/s) rather than memhier's slower loop (0.77 pJ/B, 6.2 TB/s),
  so ET's on-chip energy advantage over the A100 is 15–24×, not 15–17×. `mmbench-report-data.py` now stores the
  matmul watts to enough digits that a regeneration reproduces the page. Then: every page rebuilt from its
  generator byte for byte; `final_links.py` on the 17 public pages, 0 problems; `check_page.sh` on all 18 at 1280
  and 390 px in light and dark, all OK; the deploy (all 18 live pages were still the `08076ae` versions), then
  `spacesheep list` (no slug or visibility changed), each public URL 200, the private brief not served anonymously,
  the three Horace GIFs 200, and each live page equal to its repo file apart from the host's `data-ss-id` attributes.

## Publishing notes, learned the hard way

- Deploy with `spacesheep deploy <dir> --space <uuid> -m "<message>"` from a directory of its own (MIRROR.md,
  "Deploying one page"). **Always pass `--space`** when updating, or the CLI creates a new space.
- **Deploy A4 as its folder, never as a single file.** The GIFs (A6–A8) are part of that space, and a single-file
  deploy drops them (it happened on 2026-09-24; the folder was redeployed the same day).
- A folder deploy (`index.html` plus assets, which is how A4 ships its GIFs) has **changed a space's
  visibility in both directions**. Run `spacesheep list` after every deploy and fix it with
  `spacesheep share <uuid> --visibility public|private`. The visibility column above is a snapshot, and
  visibility is the repo owner's call, not the deploy script's.
- 🛑 **An agent never changes an existing page's tier, even to the tier the rules say it should have had.**
  `AGENT.md` §4 lists it under "Steps only a person can do": *"any change to an existing page's visibility,
  which is the owner's"*. On **26 October 2026** a Q126 session found the E63 camera page (`5aed67f2`) sitting
  at `signed_in` although MIRROR.md had read `public` since 23 October and §8's rule makes a report page
  public; it read the live page for keys, addresses, people and NDA facts, found it clean, and moved it to
  public itself. The tier is almost certainly the right one and nothing private went out, but the call was not
  the agent's to make and the owner was not asked. **What to do instead:** say the page's tier disagrees with
  MIRROR.md, say what the read found, and hand over the one line —
  `spacesheep share <uuid> --visibility public` — for him to run or wave through. A tier that is *too open* for
  its content is the exception: close it at once and say so.
- **Delete any `.spacesheep.json` the CLI leaves behind, every time.** It pins a folder to a space, and
  `deploy` uses it when `--space` is absent. On 2026-09-22 a stale `docs/reports/.spacesheep.json` from the
  DVFS brief silently published the hot-line report **over** that brief. The recovery is to remove the pin
  file, redeploy the right HTML with an explicit `--space`, and check the published `<title>`. Safest habit:
  deploy a new report from a directory of its own, and always pass `--space` for an update.
- A new space is private and gets its slug from the file or folder name. Pass `--title`, `--slug`, `--emoji`
  and `--description` on the first deploy, or fix them on the next one; all four are kept on later updates.
- **Check the CLI's version** (2026-09-25): `spacesheep --version`. The pages up to 25 September were deployed with
  CLI 1.5.1, a GitHub build installed on aifoundry2 while npm's latest was 1.2.1 (so `npx --yes spacesheep` fetched an
  older CLI than the one used, and `spacesheep update` downgraded). On the evening of 25 September npm's latest
  became 1.9.1 and aifoundry2's CLI was updated to it; `check-mirror.py` gives the same result with both. The CLI's
  key lives in `~/.config/spacesheep/` and never goes into the repository.
- **Check that live equals repo after every deploy** with `python3 scripts/check-mirror.py` (see
  [`../reports/MIRROR.md`](../reports/MIRROR.md)). What it learned about the host on 2026-09-25: `spacesheep read <uuid>
  index.html -o <dir>` returns the stored page with `data-ss-id` attributes added to elements; the raw address
  `https://<uuid>.spacesheep.app/` also inserts the host's print style, print mark and viewer scripts (16,757 bytes,
  the same on every page) just before `</body>`; `spacesheep read` returns binary files (the GIFs) mangled, so fetch
  those over HTTPS; and the raw address answers Python's default user agent with 403.
- **A private page is never mirrored** into this public repository: not its file, and not its address while its
  visibility is in doubt. MIRROR.md lists private pages by name with the reason.
- **On an update, `--title` without `--slug` re-slugs the space.** On 2026-09-24 the review's redeploy passed
  new titles and every one of the 18 spaces moved to a slug made from its title (the old URLs went to a sign-in
  page) for about seven minutes, until each was redeployed with `--slug <old slug>`. Pass the existing `--slug`
  whenever you pass `--title`, and check `spacesheep list` for the URLs afterwards.
- **An animated page that "jumps": measure it frame by frame** (30 September) with `tools/pagemotion/`, one run at
  a time, with a Chrome profile kept between runs (a fresh profile compiles GPU shaders at every first paint, 50–150 ms
  spikes that a returning visitor does not see). Late frames and a camera path that turns or changes pace at a scale
  both read as jumps; `kink.py` separates the two. Stroked text (knockout halos) re-rasterises at every scale of a
  zoom: drop the stroke while the camera moves.
- **`spacesheep list` shows only the 50 most recently updated spaces** (found 5 October 2026). The CLI (1.9.1, and the
  current 1.26.0 alike) calls the host's `list_spaces` once, without a limit or an offset, and prints that first page,
  newest first; the reply's `total` and `next_offset` are dropped, there is no flag for more, and nothing says the list
  is cut. `--json` is cut the same way. On 5 October the account had 469 spaces and the 50th listed had last changed
  about a day and a half earlier: pages that cron jobs republish daily push every older page off the list. So a space
  missing from `spacesheep list` is **not** a deleted space. What it broke: `scripts/check-mirror.py` reported 38 of
  MIRROR.md's rows as "space not in `spacesheep list`" and exited 1, and for those rows it could not check the live
  visibility and slug; its warning about a public space that MIRROR.md does not list saw only the newest 50; the
  dashboard's guard (`tools/lab/dashboard/update.sh`, `vis_list`) reads the same list, which is safe only while the
  dashboard republishes at least hourly. Still fine: checking a page right after its own deploy (a deploy lists it
  first). Until the CLI pages through the list, trust check-mirror's `equal` lines and its anonymous checks, and read
  a missing row as unchecked; the host's `list_spaces` (the MCP tool) takes `limit` (at most 200) and `offset` and
  reports `next_offset`, which reaches every space. Reported to the spacesheep team the same day
  (https://spacesheep.dev/feedback?thread=a80049a9-14ae-41d8-9cc9-c4472925c34d).
- **The spacesheep viewer does not pass its `#…` into the page** (30 September): a link such as
  `/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-memory-levels#dram/load` opens the page's default view; the raw address
  `https://<uuid>.spacesheep.app/#…` keeps it. Link to a view with the raw address.
- **Movies on a page: no native video controls over a burnt-in key** (22 October 2026, E62). A paused
  `<video controls>` gets the browser's control bar and a dark gradient over its bottom 60–90 px, which is where
  `tools/claims-v3/hm/render_hm.py` burns the key, the schedule strip and the timeline; on a phone the bar (play, 0:00,
  menu) covers them outright. The HTML looks fine; a full-page screenshot shows it at once. The E62 page
  (`docs/reports/2026-10-21-heat-movies/`) keeps `controls` in its HTML for readers without scripts, and its script
  swaps them for one play/pause button over the picture: each movie plays muted on a loop while half of it is on
  screen, pauses when scrolled away, and starts nothing under reduced motion.
- **A page with a stream reads blank in screenshots** (22 October 2026): the MCP `screenshot` tool loads the raw
  address, where a stream cannot play and the player says so ("Can't show thermal/p3"). Check the stream itself with
  the MCP `streams` tool and its `path` (the last values say `live: true`). Such a page is served with the host's
  streams client right after its `<meta name="ss-streams">` tag, which `check-mirror.py` removes since 22 October.
- **`check-mirror.py` needs CA certificates** (22 October 2026): python.org's macOS framework builds (the Intel's
  `python3`, 3.7.9) ship without a CA bundle until their `Install Certificates.command` runs, and then every page reads
  `unreachable` with `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED`, which looks like an outage. On a Mac, run it with
  `SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/ssl/cert.pem`.
- **A new space takes the account's default tier, not the rule, and `MIRROR.md` can say otherwise for days**
  (26 October 2026, Q126). The E63 page `et-soc1-bitflip-camera` was created on 23 October with no `visibility`
  argument, so it took the account's own default for new pages and came up `signed_in`, while §8.4's rule makes a lab
  report public, its MIRROR row said `public`, and three hubs linked it as public. Nobody saw it, because
  `check-mirror.py` could not check the row's visibility: the space was outside the 50 that `spacesheep list` returns.
  **Pass `--visibility public` (or the MCP `access.visibility`) on every new report's first deploy**, and read the
  tier back with `get_space` rather than trusting the MIRROR row. Re-tiering a live space is `share_space`, never a
  redeploy: `deploy` ignores `access.visibility` on an update, so a page deployed again keeps the tier it has.
- **A rebuilt reduction is not always byte-identical** (26 October 2026, Q126). E63 session 1's RED was made on the
  Intel and not kept; rebuilt on aifoundry2 from the same raw capture with the same `reduce_hm.py`, it reads the same
  48,005 packets and 3,841 bins but finds 244 shutter events against the committed `ffc.json`'s 243 — one borderline
  step on the other side of the 40 mK threshold under a different numpy. Keep the RED of anything published, or say in
  the data README which numbers a rebuild moves (here: one no-verdict amplitude, 13.4 → 14.2 mK against an SE of 47).
